shows that WDRO consistently achieves lower prediction errors than ERM across all regression settings, with more significant improvements under perturbed test sets, reaching approximately 10%. This pattern holds across all three loss functions, indicating WDRO exhibits greater robustness under distributional shifts. Table
2
shows that WDRO consistently outperforms ERM across all shift probabilities and test settings in classification tasks. Although the performance gap decreases as the shift probability increases, WDRO still maintains a clear advantage, particularly in imbalanced and perturbed scenarios.

6.2
Application to MNIST dataset

We evaluated the WDRO method on the MNIST dataset
[
30
]
, available through PyTorch’s
torchvision
library. For computational efficiency, we subsampled
3000
training and
600
test samples from the original dataset. The network architecture followed a modified LeNet design, consisting of two convolutional layers (with
6
and
16
channels,
5\times 5
kernels, ReLU activations, and
2\times 2
max pooling) and three fully connected layers (with
120
,
84
, and
10
units, respectively). The model outputs logits for
10
-class classification, and the cross-entropy loss is applied during training to optimize the model.

To simulate real-world distortions, we applied three types of perturbations: (i) Occlusion: randomly masks a
12\times 12
region of the image by setting pixel values to zero, simulating localized information loss;
(ii) Corner corruption: adds a fixed bright patch (pixel value 1) to the top-left corner, mimicking structured noise;
(iii) Pixel-wise noise: adds Gaussian noise to each pixel with a noise level of
0.3
, clipping the values to the range
[0,1]
.
Additionally, all perturbations incorporated a label-shift strategy, replacing certain digits with visually similar alternatives based on common confusion patterns
(3\leftrightarrow 5\leftrightarrow 8,\ 1\leftrightarrow 2\leftrightarrow 7)
. Perturbations were applied to randomly selected samples with shift probabilities from
\{0.1,0.2,0.3\}
. The examples of the three types of perturbations are shown in Figure
1
.

Both WDRO and ERM methods were trained on perturbed data and evaluated on both standard (unperturbed) and perturbed test sets, with the latter generated using twice the training shift probability. The WDRO uncertainty level was selected via cross-validation over
\{0.0,0.1,0.2,0.3\}
. Each experiment was repeated independently 20 times.

(a)
Occlusion with similar label shift.

(b)
Corner corruption with similar label shift.

(c)
Pixel-wise noise with similar label shift.

Fig 1:
Examples of the three types of perturbations applied during training and evaluation.

shows that WDRO consistently outperforms ERM under both standard and perturbed test sets across nearly all perturbation types and shift probabilities.
In particular, WDRO exhibits enhanced robustness in the corner corruption setting, with an average performance gain exceeding 0.9%. In contrast, the improvement under occlusion perturbation is more modest, averaging around 0.5%.

7
Discussion

In this paper, we develop a general analytical framework for studying the generalization performance of estimators derived from the local worst-case risk under model misspecification. We show that the local worst-case risk is closely connected to the regularized natural risk, which implicitly enforces gradient regularization, and we provide explicit bounds for this relationship when different
W_{k}
are considered. The results for the
k=1
case further illustrate the distinction between the local worst-case risk and the adversarial risk, with the former mainly influenced by points where the Lipschitz constant is attained and the latter shaped by local behavior. We propose a distributionally robust nonparametric estimator by minimizing the local worst-case risk within a norm-constrained FNN space. The norm constraint plays an important role by providing both a Lipschitz upper bound and a uniform bound on the Hessian norm. By jointly analyzing the function and its gradient, we establish bounds on the space’s complexity and approximation properties. This fundamental property is particularly important for the analysis in the
k>1
case. The main theoretical result establishes non-asymptotic error bounds for the excess local worst-case risk. With an appropriate choice of
\delta
, the estimator achieves the minimax optimal rate when
\alpha<(d+2m+1)/2
. The bounds also precisely characterize the dependence of the robustness-related term, showing a linear structure for
k=1
and a higher-order dependence for
k>1
. The assumptions for this analysis are verified in typical nonparametric regression and classification problems. Finally, numerical comparisons in simulations and applications to the MNIST dataset demonstrate the superior robustness of the proposed estimator compared to its natural risk-based counterpart.

Several important problems remain for further study. Developing higher-order approximation theories for norm-constrained FNNs will deepen the understanding of their expressive power. It is also of interest to investigate ambiguity balls tailored to specific application settings to enhance practical relevance and performance.

8
Supplementary Material

The Supplementary Material is organized as follows. Section
A
introduces auxiliary lemmas, with their proofs provided in Section
D
. Section
B
presents the proofs for the lemmas from the main text, and Section
C
contains the proofs for the theorems. For clarity, the original statements of these results are restated before their respective proofs. Finally, Section
E
describes the algorithm used in the numerical study presented in Section 6 of the main text.

Notations.

We first outline some notations to be used in the sequel. Let the set of positive integers be denoted by
\mathbb{N}=\{1,2,...\}
and let
\mathbb{N}_{0}=\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}.
For a integer
d
, let
[d]=\{1,\dots,d\}.
For any interger
q\in\mathbb{N}
, let
q^{\star}
be the integer such that
1/q+1/q^{\star}=1
.
If
a
and
b
are two quantities, we use
a\lesssim b
or
b\gtrsim a
to denote the statement that
a\leq Cb
for some constant
C>0
. We denote
a\asymp b
when
a\lesssim b\lesssim a
. Let
\lceil a\rceil
denote the smallest integer larger than or equal to quantity
a
. For a vector
\bm{x}
and
p\in[1,\infty]
, we use
\|\bm{x}\|_{p}
to denote the
p
-norm of
\bm{x}
. For a function
f
, we denote its supremum norm by
\|f\|_{\infty}
and its gradient by
\nabla f
. For integer
p>0
, define
\|f\|_{p}=\{\mathbb{E}[|f(X)|^{p}]\}^{1/p}
and
\|f\|_{p,n}=\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|f(X_{i})|^{p}\}^{1/p}
.
When it is necessary to emphasize the distribution of
X
, we write
\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{P})}=\{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[|f(X)|^{p}]\}^{1/p}.

For a function
f
defined on a set
\Omega
, its
L^{\infty}
norm is defined as

\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}=\inf\{C\geq 0:|f(x)|\leq C\ \text{for almost all }x\in\Omega\}.

For a measurable function
H
, let
\esssup(H)
denote its essential supremum.

A
Auxiliary lemmas

The following result follows directly from Example 5.8 of
[
54
]
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\|\cdot\|
be a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and denote by
\mathbb{B}^{d}=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{x}\|\leq 1\}
the corresponding unit ball. Then,

(1/u)^{d}\leq\mathcal{N}(u,\mathbb{B}^{d},\|\cdot\|)\leq(2/u+1)^{d}.

The following lemma provides an upper bound for the covering number of a class of bounded matrices, with the proof deferred to Section
D
.

Lemma A.2
.

Define the set

\mathcal{M}(m,n,r)=\Big\{\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}:\|\bm{W}\|_{\infty}\leq r\Big\},

where
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denotes the infinity matrix norm satisfying
\|\bm{W}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{\|\bm{x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\|\bm{W}\bm{x}\|_{\infty}
.
The
u
-covering number associated with the infinity matrix norm for the class
\mathcal{M}(m,n,r)
satisfies

\mathcal{N}\left(u,\mathcal{M}(m,n,r),\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)\leq(2r/u+1)^{mn}.

The following lemma shows any
g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S})
can be reparameterized so that each weight matrix in the hidden layers has norm at most one, with proof deferred to Section
D
.

Lemma A.3
.

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
q\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. For every
g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S})
, there exists a reparameterization

g(\bm{x})=\tilde{g}_{L}\circ\tilde{g}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ\tilde{g}_{0}(\bm{x}),

where
\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\bm{x}+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})
for
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
, and
\tilde{g}_{L}(\bm{x})=\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\bm{x}
. The parameters satisfy
\|(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\|_{q}\leq 1
for
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
, and

\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}=\bm{A}_{L}(\prod_{s=0}^{L-1}r_{s}^{m^{L-s}}),
with
r_{s}=\max\{\|(\bm{A}_{s},\bm{b}_{s})\|_{q},1\}.

Lemma A.4
.

Let
g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S})
be an FNN with RePU activation
\sigma_{m}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}
, and assume the hidden-layer weights and biases are normalized so that
\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
. Let
\mathcal{X}=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}
. Then, the gradient and Hessian of
g
satisfy

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla g(\bm{x})\|_{1}\leq m^{L}\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\quad\text{ and }\quad\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\big\|\nabla^{2}g(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq Lm^{2L}\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}.

Define two classes of functions over the unit ball
\mathbb{B}^{d}=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{x}\|_{2}\leq 1\}
as follows.

\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K):=\Bigl\{f(\bm{x})=\sum_{i=1}^{W}a_{i}\,\sigma_{m}((\bm{x}^{\top},1)\bm{v}_{i}):\bm{v}_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{d},\;\sum_{i=1}^{W}|a_{i}|\leq K\Bigr\},

\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(K):=\left\{f(\bm{x})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d}}\sigma_{m}((\bm{x}^{\top},1)\bm{v})d\mu(\bm{v}):\|\mu\|\leq K\right\},

where
\|\mu\|
denotes the total variation and
\mathbb{S}^{d}=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}:\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\}
. The following lemma, based on Theorems 2 and 3 of
[
45
]
, establishes that
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(K)
admits a simultaneous approximation in both function values and derivatives by
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
0\leq r\leq m
. Then there exists a constant

C=C(d,m)>0
such that for any
W\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(1)}\;\inf_{g\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,1)}\Big\{\;\sup_{\|\bm{s}\|_{1}\leq r}\big\|D^{\bm{s}}(f-g)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\;\Big\}\leq C(d,m)W^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2(m-r)+1}{2d}}.

The next lemma shows properties of the operator
S_{m}
, introduced in
[
4
,
56
]
, which plays an important role in the proof of the approximation result stated in Theorem 3.4 of the main text. The detailed proof of the lemma can be found in Section
D
.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
h\in C^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d})
and define the operator
S_{m}:C^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{d})\mapsto C^{1}(\mathbb{B}^{d})
as

S_{m}(h)(\bm{x})=(1+\|\bm{x}\|_{2}^{2})^{m/2}h\big(\bm{u}(\bm{x})\big),\quad\text{with }\;\bm{u}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\|\bm{x}\|_{2}^{2}}}\begin{pmatrix}\bm{x}\\
1\end{pmatrix}\;\text{ for }\;\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}.

Then

\|S_{m}(h)\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\leq 2^{m/2}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}
. For
1\leq i\neq j\leq d+1
, define the angular derivative

D_{i,j}=u_{i}\partial_{u_{j}}-u_{j}\partial_{u_{i}}.
There exists a constant
C>0
, depending only on
m
and
d
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\|\nabla_{\bm{x}}S_{m}(h)(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq C\;\Big\{\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\|D_{i,j}h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\Big\}.

Next, we introduce several fundamental tools from empirical process theory to support the main analysis. Specifically, for a given function class
\mathcal{F}
and a radius
r>0
, the localized empirical Rademacher complexities under
\|\cdot\|_{2}
and
\|\cdot\|_{2,n}
are respectively defined as

\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}):=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(\bm{x}_{i})\Big|\Big],\;\quad\;\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}):=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}\\
\|g\|_{2,n}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(\bm{x}_{i})\Big|\Big],

where
\bm{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{n})^{\top}
denotes a vector of independent Rademacher variables, independent of the samples
\{\bm{x}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.
If
\mathcal{F}
is star-shaped, i.e.,
ag\in\mathcal{F}
for every
g\in\mathcal{F}
and
a\in[0,1]
, then the ratio
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F})/r
is non-increasing in
r
. Specifically, this property is stated in the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to Section
D
.

Lemma A.7
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a star-shaped function class. Then, for any
0<r_{1}\leq r_{2}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r_{2}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r_{2};\mathcal{F})\leq\frac{1}{r_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r_{1};\mathcal{F}).

For completeness, we restate several key inequalities from empirical process theory.

Lemma A.8
(Dudley’s integral covering number bound
[
47
]
).

Suppose the function class
\mathcal{F}
satisfies
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\|f\|_{2,n}\leq B
for some positive constant
B
. Then,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}f(X_{i})\Big\}\leq\inf_{\eta\geq 0}\Big\{4\eta+12\int_{\eta}^{B}\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{F},L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}))}{n}}du\Big\},\end{split}

where
\bm{\sigma}=(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{n})^{\top}
denotes a vector of independent Rademacher variables, independent of the samples.

Lemma A.9
(Ledoux–Talagrand contraction inequality
[
31
]
).

Let
F:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a convex and increasing function.
For each
i\in[n]
, let
\varphi_{i}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function with Lipschitz constant
1
and
\varphi_{i}(0)=0
. Then, for any bounded subset
T\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\mathbb{E}F\left(\frac{1}{2}\sup_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})\in T}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\varphi_{i}\left(t_{i}\right)\right|\right)\leq\mathbb{E}F\left(\sup_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})\in T}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}t_{i}\right|\right).

Lemma A.10
(Talagrand concentration for empirical processes
[
54
]
).

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a countable class of functions satisfying
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\|f\|_{\infty}\leq B
for some constant
B
. Define

Z=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{i})\right\}~~\text{ or }~~Z=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(X_{i})\right|.

Then, for any
\delta>0
, it holds that

P\Big\{Z\geq\mathbb{E}[Z]+\delta\Big\}\leq 2\exp\left(\frac{-n\delta^{2}}{8e\mathbb{E}\left[\Sigma^{2}\right]+4B\delta}\right).

Here,
\Sigma^{2}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f^{2}\left(X_{i}\right)
, and its expectation satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\Sigma^{2}]\leq\sigma^{2}+2B\mathbb{E}[Z]
, where
\sigma^{2}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}[f^{2}\left(X\right)]
.

Lemma A.11
(McDiarmid’s inequality).

Let
f:\mathcal{X}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfy the bounded differences property with constants
c_{i}
, i.e., for each
i\in[n]
, all
(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\in\mathcal{X}^{n}
, and any
x_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
,

\left|f\left(x_{1},\dots,x_{i-1},x_{i},x_{i+1},\dots,x_{n}\right)-f\left(x_{1},\dots,x_{i-1},x_{i}^{\prime},x_{i+1},\dots,x_{n}\right)\right|\leq c_{i}.

Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
be independent random variables. Then, for any
t>0
,

P\Big\{\Big|f\left(X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[f\left(X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\right)\right]\Big|\geq t\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{2t^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}c_{i}^{2}}\Big\}.

The next lemma is instrumental in establishing the convergence rates of our main results. Its proof, guided by the localization techniques in Chapter 14 of
[
54
]
, is deferred to Section
D
.

Lemma A.12
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{n}
be a class of functions satisfying
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
, where
M_{n}
is a positive constant that may depend on
n
, and define its star-shaped shifted class as

\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}:=\{a(f-f_{0}):a\in[0,1],f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\},
where the reference function
f_{0}
also satisfies
\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
. Let
r_{n}
be defined such that
\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]=r_{n}^{2}/(64M_{n}).
For any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, let
H_{n}(f;f_{0}):=\mathbb{E}[\ell(Z;f)-\ell(Z;f_{0})]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{\ell(Z_{i};f)-\ell(Z_{i};f_{0})\}
, where
\ell(\bm{z};f)=\ell(f(\bm{x})-y)
with
\bm{z}=(\bm{x},y)
. Then there exist positive constants

b_{1},b_{2}
such that, with probability at least

1-2\exp\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}+b_{2}\log(\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1}))\}
, we have

\big|H_{n}(f;f_{0})\big|\leq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}(\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}+r_{n})~~~~\text{ for any $f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}$}.

We note that Lemma
A.12
extends beyond the specific loss
\ell(f(\bm{x})-y)
. The result holds for any loss of the form
\ell(\bm{z};f)=\phi(f(\bm{x}),y)
that is
L
-Lipschitz with respect to its function argument, i.e.,

|\ell(\bm{z};f)-\ell(\bm{z};\tilde{f})|\leq L|f(\bm{x})-\tilde{f}(\bm{x})|\quad\text{for some }L>0.

In such a generalization, the term
\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}
in the lemma’s statement would be replaced by
L
. However, for consistency with the primary setting of the main text, we present Lemma
A.12
in its current, more specific form.

As analyzing the localized empirical Rademacher complexity
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})
is often more tractable than
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]
, we establish a connection between the two in the following lemma. The proof, guided by
[
54
]
, is deferred to Section
D
. Together with Theorem 3.3 in the main text, this lemma further implies Lemma
A.14
.

Lemma A.13
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
is a star-shaped function class satisfying
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}}\|g\|_{\infty}\leq 2M_{n}
,
where
M_{n}
is a positive constant that may depend on
n
.
Let
\hat{r}_{n}
and
r_{n}
be positive numbers such that
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(\hat{r}_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\leq c\hat{r}_{n}^{2}
for a positive constant
c
, and
\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]=r_{n}^{2}/(64M_{n})
. Assume
r_{n}/M_{n}=o(1)
. Then there exist positive constants
s_{1},s_{2}
such that

\hat{r}_{n}\geq s_{1}r_{n}/(cM_{n})

with probability at least
1-\exp\{-\frac{nr_{n}^{4}}{s_{2}M_{n}^{4}}\}
.

Lemma A.14
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{n}=\{T_{M_{n}}g:g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)\}
and
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}=\{a(f-f_{0}):a\in[0,1],f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\}
, where
M_{n}
is a positive constant that may depend on
n
. Assume that the reference function
f_{0}
satisfies
\|f_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
,
and that
M_{n}\lesssim K
and
\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)/n=o(1)
. For
r_{n}
satisfying
\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]=r_{n}^{2}/(64M_{n})
, there exists a positive constant
c
such that

r_{n}\leq cM_{n}n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)}.

Lemma A.15
.

For any
a,b\geq 0
and
r\in(0,1]
, we have

|a^{r}-b^{r}|\;\leq\;|a-b|^{r}.

B
Proof of lemmas

uppose the composed loss
\ell(\cdot;f)
has a bounded Lipschitz constant
\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}<\infty
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)\leq\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

Furthermore, there holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)=\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}},

if one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(i)

There exists
\bm{z}_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}
such that

{\lim\sup}_{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\ell(\bm{z};f)-\ell(\bm{z}_{0};f)}{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|}=\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

(ii)

There exists
\bm{z}_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}
, a positive constant
\tau
, and a sequence
\{r_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}\rightarrow 0
such that

{\sup}_{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\geq\tau}\frac{\ell(\bm{z};f)-\ell(\bm{z}_{0};f)}{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|}=\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}

and
P(\|Z-\bm{z}_{0}\|\leq r_{m})>0
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
.

remark

From the definition of the Wasserstein distance, the worst-case risk
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)
can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)=\sup_{\tilde{Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Z}):\mathbb{E}[\|\tilde{Z}-Z\|]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{Z};f)]=\sup_{\Delta\in\mathcal{P}_{1}(\mathcal{Z}):\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}[\ell(Z+\Delta;f)].

It directly follows an upper bound of
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;0)
based on the definition of the Lipschitz constant.

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;0)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z+\Delta;f)-\ell(Z;f)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\|\Delta\|\big]\leq\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

Moreover, under the conditions (i) or (ii), there exists
\bm{z}_{0}
and a sequence
\{\bm{z}_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
such that

\frac{\ell(\bm{z}_{m};f)-\ell(\bm{z}_{0};f)}{\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|}\geq\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}-m^{-1}.

Define
\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})=\{\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}:\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\leq r_{m}\}
for a positive nonincreasing sequence
\{r_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
. It is required that
P(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0}))>0
and
\{r_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
will be specified subsequently.
For any
\bm{z}\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0}),
we have

\begin{split}\ell(\bm{z}_{m};f)-\ell(\bm{z};f)&\geq\ell(\bm{z}_{m};f)-\ell(\bm{z}_{0};f)-\big|\ell(\bm{z}_{0};f)-\ell(\bm{z};f)\big|\\
&\geq\big\{\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}-m^{-1}\big\}\big\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\big\|-\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\big\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\big\|\\
&\geq\big\{\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}-m^{-1}\big\}\big\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}\big\|-2\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\big\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\big\|\\
&\geq\big\{\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}-m^{-1}\big\}\big\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}\big\|-2\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}r_{m}.\end{split}

(13)

Consider a sequence of random vectors defined by

Z_{m}=\{(1-\pi_{m})Z+\pi_{m}\bm{z}_{m}\}1\{Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\}+Z1\{Z\notin\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\},

where
\pi_{m}\in\{0,1\}
is independent of
Z
satisfying
P(\pi_{m}=1)=\varepsilon_{m}
for some
\varepsilon_{m}\in(0,1)
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|Z_{m}-Z\|\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\|Z_{m}-Z\|\mid\pi_{m}=1\big]P(\pi_{m}=1)+\mathbb{E}\big[\|Z_{m}-Z\|\mid\pi_{m}=0\big]P(\pi_{m}=0)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{m}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{z}_{m}1\{Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\}+Z1\{Z\notin\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\}-Z\|\big]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{m}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\bm{z}_{m}-Z\|1\{Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\varepsilon_{m}(\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|-r_{m})P\big(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\big).

Select
\varepsilon_{m}
such that
\mathbb{E}\big[\|Z_{m}-Z\|\big]=\delta
. Then, it follows from (
13
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z_{m};f)\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z;f)\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z_{m};f)-\ell(Z;f)\mid\pi_{m}=1\big]P(\pi_{m}=1)+\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z_{m};f)-\ell(Z;f)\mid\pi_{m}=0\big]P(\pi_{m}=0)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{m}\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(\bm{z}_{m}1\{Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\}+Z1\{Z\notin\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\};f)-\ell(Z;f)\big]

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{m}\mathbb{E}\big[\{\ell(\bm{z}_{m};f)-\ell(Z;f)\}1\{Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\big\{\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}-m^{-1}\big\}\varepsilon_{m}\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|\bm{z}_{m}-Z\big\|1\{Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\}\big]-2\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\varepsilon_{m}r_{m}P\big(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\big)

\displaystyle=\delta\big\{\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}-m^{-1}\big\}-2\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\varepsilon_{m}r_{m}P\big(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\big).

In addition, the following condition

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{m}r_{m}P\big(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\big)\leq\frac{r_{m}P\big(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\big)\delta}{(\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|-r_{m})P\big(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0})\big)}\leq\frac{r_{m}\delta}{(\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|-r_{m})}\rightarrow 0

can be satisfied if
\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\rightarrow\infty
and
r_{m}
is selected as a fixed value, or when
\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\geq\tau
for some
\tau>0
and
r_{m}\rightarrow 0.
In such case,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;0)\geq\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z_{m};f)\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z;f)\big]\quad\text{ for all }m\in\mathbb{N}.

Therefore, we show

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;0)=\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

The first case that
\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\rightarrow\infty
is satisfied under the condition (i), that is,

{\lim\sup}_{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\ell(\bm{z};f)-\ell(\bm{z}_{0};f)}{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|}=\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

The second case that
\|\bm{z}_{m}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\geq\tau
for some
\tau>0
and
r_{m}\rightarrow 0
is satisfied under the condition (ii), that is,

{\sup}_{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|\geq\tau}\frac{\ell(\bm{z};f)-\ell(\bm{z}_{0};f)}{\|\bm{z}-\bm{z}_{0}\|}=\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}

and
P(Z\in\mathbb{F}_{m}(\bm{z}_{0}))>0
. Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

uppose there exists a positive number
q>0
and a measuable function
H(\bm{z};f)
such that for any
\tilde{\bm{z}},\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z},
there holds

\|\nabla\ell(\tilde{\bm{z}};f)-\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\|_{1}\leq H(\bm{z};f)\|\tilde{\bm{z}}-\bm{z}\|_{\infty}^{q}.

Let
\mathbb{A}=\{\bm{z}:\|\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\|_{1}=\esssup(\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1})\}
. Suppose
\mathbb{E}[H(Z;f)1\{Z\in\mathbb{A}\}]<\infty
and
P(Z\in\mathbb{A})>0
, then

\displaystyle\delta\esssup(\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1})-\delta^{1+q}\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)1\{Z\in\mathbb{A}\}\big]}{P(Z\in\mathbb{A})^{1+q}}\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)\leq\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

remark

First, the upper bound can be derived from Lemma 2.3 in the main text. Next, to derive the lower bound, define
\mathbb{A}=\{\bm{z}:\|\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\|_{1}=\esssup(\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1})\}
as the set of points where the norm of
\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)
reaches its essential supremum. Here,
\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)
denotes the gradient of
\ell(\bm{z};f)
with respect to
\bm{z}.
Define

\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}=\frac{\delta\operatorname{sgn}(\nabla\ell(Z;f))1\{Z\in\mathbb{A}\}}{P(Z\in\mathbb{A})},

where
\operatorname{sgn}(\cdot)
is applied element-wise and satisfies
\operatorname{sgn}(x)=x/|x|
for
x\neq 0
and
\operatorname{sgn}(x)=0
when
x=0
.
Then, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\infty}]\leq\delta
and
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)]=\delta\esssup(\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}).
This in fact indicates that
\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}
is selected such that
\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}[\Delta^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)]
is reached, which is upper bounded by
\delta\esssup(\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1})
due to Hölder’s inequality. It then follows

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;0)=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z+\Delta;f)-\ell(Z;f)\big]

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)dt\big]

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}]\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)+\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\big[\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)+\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta}_{1};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]

\displaystyle\geq\delta\esssup(\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1})-\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta}_{1};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}\big|\big]dt.

To bound the second term in the last inequality, note that the growth condition for the gradient of the loss function implies that

\displaystyle\|\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta}_{1};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}\leq H(Z;f)\|t\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\infty}^{q},

from which we derive

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta}_{1};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}\big|\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta}_{1};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\infty}H(Z;f)\|t\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\infty}^{q}\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\infty}^{1+q}\big]\leq\delta^{1+q}\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)1\{Z\in\mathbb{A}\}\big]}{P(Z\in\mathbb{A})^{1+q}}.

Therefore, from
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;0)=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)
, we derive

\displaystyle\delta\esssup(\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1})-\delta^{1+q}\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)1\{Z\in\mathbb{A}\}\big]}{P(Z\in\mathbb{A})^{1+q}}\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)\leq\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

uppose there exists a measuable function
H(\bm{z};f)
such that for any
\tilde{\bm{z}},\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z},
there holds

\big\|\nabla\ell(\tilde{\bm{z}};f)-\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\big\|_{1}\leq H(\bm{z};f)\big\|\tilde{\bm{z}}-\bm{z}\big\|_{\infty}^{\min\{1,k-1\}}.

When
k\in(2,\infty)
and
H(\bm{z};f)\in L^{\frac{k}{k-2}}(\mathbb{P})
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)-\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{1/k^{\star}}\big|\leq\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)^{\frac{k}{k-2}}\big]^{1-\frac{2}{k}}.

When
k\in(1,2]
and
H(\bm{z};f)\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{P})
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)-\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{1/k^{\star}}\big|\leq\delta^{k}\esssup\big(H(Z;f)\big).

remark

From the definition of the Wasserstein distance, the worst-case risk
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)
can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)=\sup_{\tilde{Z}\in\mathcal{P}_{k}(\mathcal{Z}):\mathbb{E}[\|\tilde{Z}-Z\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}[\ell(\tilde{Z};f)]=\sup_{\Delta\in\mathcal{P}_{k}(\mathcal{Z}):\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}[\ell(Z+\Delta;f)].

Define
k^{\star}
as the integer such that
1/k+1/k^{\star}=1
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;0)=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z+\Delta;f)-\ell(Z;f)\big]

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)dt\big]

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)+\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}+\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{\frac{1}{k^{\star}}}+\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big],

where the last two inequalities follow from Hölder’s inequality. Define

\widetilde{\Delta}=\frac{\delta\operatorname{sgn}(\nabla\ell(Z;f))}{\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{1-k^{\star}}}\mathbb{E}[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}]^{-\frac{1}{k}}.

It satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}
and
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\Delta}^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)]=\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{1/k^{\star}}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;0)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)+\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\big[\widetilde{\Delta}^{\top}\nabla\ell(Z;f)+\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{\Delta}^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]

\displaystyle\geq\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{\frac{1}{k^{\star}}}-\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\widetilde{\Delta}^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}\big|\big]dt.

When
k\in(2,\infty)
, the second term in the last inequality can be upper bounded. From Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\widetilde{\Delta}^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}\big|\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}H(Z;f)\|t\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)^{\frac{k}{k-2}}\big]^{1-\frac{2}{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}^{k}\big]^{\frac{2}{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)^{\frac{k}{k-2}}\big]^{1-\frac{2}{k}}.

Following a similar analysis, we also derive

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]\leq\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)^{\frac{k}{k-2}}\big]^{1-\frac{2}{k}}.

Consequently, from
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;0)=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)
, we derive

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)-\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{\frac{1}{k^{\star}}}\big|\leq\delta^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)^{\frac{k}{k-2}}\big]^{1-\frac{2}{k}}.

When
k\in(1,2]
, the condition
\|\nabla\ell(\tilde{\bm{z}};f)-\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\|_{1}\leq H(\bm{z};f)\|\tilde{\bm{z}}-\bm{z}\|_{\infty}^{k-1}
and Hölder’s inequality imply

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\widetilde{\Delta}^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}\big|\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla\ell(Z+t\widetilde{\Delta};f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}H(Z;f)\|t\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}^{k-1}\big]dt

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[H(Z;f)\|\widetilde{\Delta}\|_{\infty}^{k}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{k}\esssup(H(Z;f)).

Following a similar analysis, we also derive

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{k}]\leq\delta^{k}}\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{0}^{1}\Delta^{\top}\{\nabla\ell(Z+t\Delta;f)-\nabla\ell(Z;f)\}dt\big]\leq\delta^{k}\esssup(H(Z;f)),

from which it follows

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)-\delta\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{\frac{1}{k^{\star}}}\big|\leq\delta^{k}\esssup(H(Z;f)).

This completes the proof.
∎

C
Proof of theorems

et
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
be defined over
\mathcal{X}
. For any
u>0
, the following bounds hold.
(i) For the supremum norm
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
, we have

\begin{split}\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\lesssim\mathcal{S}\log\left(1+Ku^{-1}\right).\end{split}

(ii) If further
m\geq 2
, then

\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K),\rho_{\infty})\lesssim\mathcal{S}\log\left(1+LKm^{L}u^{-1}\right),

where
\rho_{\infty}
is defined as
\rho_{\infty}(g,\tilde{g})=\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\{|g(\bm{x})-\tilde{g}(\bm{x})|+\|\nabla g(\bm{x})-\nabla\tilde{g}(\bm{x})\|_{1}\}.

remark

Let
B=m^{-L}K
. By Lemma
A.3
, it suffices to study the subclass

\mathcal{T}_{m}(\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)):=\Big\{g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K):\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq B,\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq 1,\;0\leq\ell\leq L-1\Big\},

which satisfies
\mathcal{T}_{m}(\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K))=\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
. Define

\Phi_{1}:=\Big\{\phi_{1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{0}\bm{x}+\bm{b}_{0}):\|(\bm{A}_{0},\bm{b}_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq 1\Big\}.

For
1\leq\ell\leq L-1
, inductively define

\Phi_{\ell+1}:=\Big\{\phi_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{\ell}\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell}):\;\phi_{\ell}\in\Phi_{\ell},\;\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq 1\Big\}.

Then
\mathcal{T}_{m}(\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K))
can be represented as

\mathcal{T}_{m}(\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K))=\Big\{g(\bm{x})=\bm{A}_{L}\phi_{L}(\bm{x}):\phi_{L}\in\Phi_{L},\;\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq B\Big\}.

We construct layer-wise coverings as follows.

(1) We first construct the cover
\widetilde{\Phi}_{1}
for
\Phi_{1}
. From Lemma
A.2
, there exists a finite set

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}:=\big\{(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{j},\tilde{\bm{b}}_{j}):j=1,\dots,(2/t+1)^{d_{1}(d+1)}\big\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}(d_{1},d+1,1)

such that for any
\phi_{1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{0}\bm{x}+\bm{b}_{0})\in\Phi_{1}
, one can find
(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0},\tilde{\bm{b}}_{0})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
with
\|(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0},\tilde{\bm{b}}_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
satisfying

\|(\bm{A}_{0}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0},\bm{b}_{0}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq t.

Define
\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0}\bm{x}+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{0})
. Then,

\displaystyle\|\phi_{1}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m\|(\bm{A}_{0}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0},\bm{b}_{0}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{0})(\bm{x}^{\top},1)^{\top}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m\|(\bm{A}_{0}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0},\bm{b}_{0}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{0})\|_{\infty}\|(\bm{x}^{\top},1)^{\top}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq mt.

Define the set

\widetilde{\Phi}_{1}:=\big\{\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\tilde{\bm{A}}\bm{x}+\tilde{\bm{b}}):(\tilde{\bm{A}},\tilde{\bm{b}})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\big\}
.
The above analysis demonstrates that
\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
over
\mathcal{X}
and
\widetilde{\Phi}_{1}
is a
(mt)
-cover of
\Phi_{1}
. In addition, the cardinality of
\widetilde{\Phi}_{1}
satisfies

|\widetilde{\Phi}_{1}|\leq(2/t+1)^{d_{1}(d+1)}.

(2) For
1\leq\ell\leq L-1
, suppose we have constructed a set
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell}
that is a
(t\cdot m\frac{m^{\ell}-1}{m-1})
-cover of
\Phi_{\ell}
, with each element
\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell}
satisfying
\|\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Further, suppose its cardinality satisfies

|\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell}|\leq(2/t+1)^{\sum_{s=0}^{\ell-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)}.

We construct the cover for the next layer. Define

\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell+1}:=\big\{\widehat{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{\ell}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell}):\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell},\;\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq 1\big\}.

From Lemma
A.2
, there exists a finite set

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\ell+1}:=\{(\tilde{\bm{A}},\tilde{\bm{b}}):j=1,\dots,(2/t+1)^{d_{\ell+1}(d_{\ell}+1)}\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}(d_{\ell+1},d_{\ell}+1,1)

such that for any
(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})
associated with
\widehat{\phi}_{\ell+1}\in\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell+1}
, one can find
(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\ell+1}
with
\|(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
satisfying

\|(\bm{A}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\;\bm{b}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq t.

Let
\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})
. Then
\|\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m\|(\bm{A}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})(\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})^{\top},1)^{\top}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m\|(\bm{A}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\|(\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})^{\top},1)^{\top}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq mt.

Define the cover for layer
\ell+1
as

\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell+1}:=\big\{\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\tilde{\bm{A}}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\tilde{\bm{b}}):\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell},(\tilde{\bm{A}},\tilde{\bm{b}})\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\ell+1}\big\}.

The cardinality of this set satisfies

|\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell+1}|\leq|\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{\ell+1}||\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell}|\leq(2/t+1)^{d_{\ell+1}(d_{\ell}+1)}\times(2/t+1)^{\sum_{s=0}^{\ell-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)}=(2/t+1)^{\sum_{s=0}^{\ell}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)}.

Now, we show that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell+1}
is a cover for
\Phi_{\ell+1}
. For any
\phi_{\ell+1}\in\Phi_{\ell+1}
, denoted as
\phi_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{\ell}\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell})
, there exists
\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell}
such that
\|\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq t\cdot m\frac{m^{\ell}-1}{m-1}
. Let
\widehat{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{\ell}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell})
. We have shown there is a
\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{\ell+1}
such that
\|\widehat{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq mt.
Combining these errors using the triangle inequality,

\begin{split}\|\phi_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}&\leq\|\phi_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})-\widehat{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}+\|\widehat{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell+1}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq m\|\bm{A}_{\ell}(\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x}))\|_{\infty}+mt\\
&\leq m\|\bm{A}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\|\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}+mt\\
&\leq m\left(t\cdot m\frac{m^{\ell}-1}{m-1}\right)+mt=t\cdot m\frac{m^{\ell+1}-1}{m-1}.\end{split}

Thus, the inductive hypothesis holds.

(3) Now we construct the cover for the FNN class. For any
g\in\mathcal{T}_{m}(\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K))
, it can be represented by

g(\bm{x})=\bm{A}_{L}\phi_{L}(\bm{x}),\quad\text{for }\phi_{L}\in\Phi_{L}\text{ and }\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq B.

Since
\widetilde{\Phi}_{L}
is a
(t\cdot m\frac{m^{L}-1}{m-1})
-cover of
\Phi_{L}
, there exists
\tilde{\phi}_{L}\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{L}
with
\|\tilde{\phi}_{L}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\|\tilde{\phi}_{L}(\bm{x})-\phi_{L}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq t\cdot m\frac{m^{L}-1}{m-1}
.
From Lemma
A.2
, there exists a finite set

\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{L+1}:=\{\tilde{\bm{A}}_{j}:j=1,\dots,(2B/\nu+1)^{d_{L}}\}\subseteq\mathcal{M}(1,d_{L},B)

such that for any
\bm{A}_{L}
satisfying
\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq B
, one can find
\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{L+1}
with
\|\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq B
satisfying

\|\bm{A}_{L}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq\nu.

Let
\tilde{g}(\bm{x})=\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\tilde{\phi}_{L}(\bm{x})
. Then, we have

\begin{split}\|g-\tilde{g}\|_{\infty}&=\|\bm{A}_{L}\phi_{L}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\tilde{\phi}_{L}\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq\|\bm{A}_{L}\phi_{L}-\bm{A}_{L}\tilde{\phi}_{L}\|_{\infty}+\|\bm{A}_{L}\tilde{\phi}_{L}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\tilde{\phi}_{L}\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq B\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\phi_{L}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{L}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}+\|\bm{A}_{L}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\|_{\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\tilde{\phi}_{L}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\\
&\leq B\left(t\cdot m\frac{m^{L}-1}{m-1}\right)+\nu.\end{split}

(14)

Define the final cover set
\widetilde{\Phi}:=\big\{\tilde{g}(\bm{x})=\tilde{\bm{A}}\tilde{\phi}_{L}(\bm{x}):\tilde{\phi}_{L}\in\widetilde{\Phi}_{L},\tilde{\bm{A}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{L+1}\big\}
. Its cardinality satisfies

|\widetilde{\Phi}|\leq|\widetilde{\Phi}_{L}||\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{L+1}|\leq(2/t+1)^{\sum_{s=0}^{L-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)}(2B/\nu+1)^{d_{L}}.

(15)

Let
\nu=\frac{u}{2}
and
Btm\frac{m^{L}-1}{m-1}=\frac{u}{2}
. This implies
t=\frac{um^{L-1}(m-1)}{2K(m^{L}-1)}
. Therefore, we show

\begin{split}&\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K),\|\cdot\|_{\infty})\\
&\leq\sum_{s=0}^{L-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)\log\left(1+\frac{4K(m^{L}-1)}{um^{L-1}(m-1)}\right)+d_{L}\log\left(1+\frac{4Km^{-L}}{u}\right)\\
&\lesssim\sum_{s=0}^{L-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)\log\left(1+K/u\right).\end{split}

Next, we derive a covering bound that controls both the function values and their gradients. Let
g\in\mathcal{T}_{m}(\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K))
, and let
\tilde{g}
be the cover element constructed in (
14
). We define their respective pre-activation vectors and gradients. For
g
, let
\phi_{0}(\bm{x})=\bm{x}
. The pre-activation vector at layer
\ell
(
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
) is defined as
\bm{z}_{\ell}(\bm{x})=\bm{A}_{\ell}\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell}
, and the transposed gradient is given by

\nabla g(\bm{x})^{\top}=\bm{A}_{L}\bm{D}_{L-1}\bm{A}_{L-1}\cdots\bm{D}_{0}\bm{A}_{0},\quad\bm{D}_{\ell}=\text{diag}(\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(z_{\ell,1}),\dots,\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(z_{\ell,d_{\ell+1}})).

Similarly, for
\tilde{g}
, let
\tilde{\phi}_{0}(\bm{x})=\bm{x}
. The pre-activation vector at layer
\ell
(
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
) is defined as
\tilde{\bm{z}}_{\ell}(\bm{x})=\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell}
.
The transposed gradient is

\nabla\tilde{g}(\bm{x})^{\top}=\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\tilde{\bm{D}}_{L-1}\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L-1}\cdots\tilde{\bm{D}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0},\quad\tilde{\bm{D}}_{\ell}=\text{diag}(\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{\ell,1}),\dots,\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{\ell,d_{\ell+1}})).

Define
\Delta\bm{A}_{\ell}=\bm{A}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}
and
\Delta\bm{D}_{\ell}=\bm{D}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{D}}_{\ell}
. The difference between the two gradients can be expressed as the telescoping sum.

\displaystyle\nabla g(\bm{x})^{\top}-\nabla\tilde{g}(\bm{x})^{\top}=

\displaystyle\big(\Delta\bm{A}_{L}\big)\big(\tilde{\bm{D}}_{L-1}\cdots\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=0}^{L-1}\big(\bm{A}_{L}\bm{D}_{L-1}\cdots\bm{A}_{\ell+1}\big)\big(\Delta\bm{D}_{\ell}\big)\big(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\cdots\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=0}^{L-1}\big(\bm{A}_{L}\bm{D}_{L-1}\cdots\bm{D}_{\ell}\big)\big(\Delta\bm{A}_{\ell}\big)\big(\tilde{\bm{D}}_{\ell-1}\cdots\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0}\big).

By the triangle inequality, the norm of each term can be bounded separately.

First, we analyze the norm of terms containing
\Delta\bm{A}_{\ell}
. For
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
, define

T_{\bm{A}_{\ell}}:=\big(\bm{A}_{L}\bm{D}_{L-1}\cdots\bm{D}_{\ell}\big)(\Delta\bm{A}_{\ell})\big(\tilde{\bm{D}}_{\ell-1}\cdots\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0}\big).

By the sub-multiplicative property of the matrix norm, together with the bounds
\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\leq B
,
\|\bm{A}_{s}\|_{\infty},\|\tilde{\bm{A}}_{s}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, and
\|\bm{D}_{s}\|_{\infty},\|\tilde{\bm{D}}_{s}\|_{\infty}\leq m
for
s\in[L-1]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|T_{\bm{A}_{\ell}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\prod_{s=\ell+1}^{L-1}(\|\bm{D}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\bm{A}_{s}\|_{\infty})\|\bm{D}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\|\Delta\bm{A}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\prod_{s=0}^{\ell-1}(\|\tilde{\bm{D}}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\tilde{\bm{A}}_{s}\|_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq Bm^{L-\ell-1}mtm^{\ell}=tBm^{L}.

Further, define
T_{\bm{A}_{L}}=(\Delta\bm{A}_{L})(\tilde{\bm{D}}_{L-1}\cdots\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0})
. Its norm satisfies

\displaystyle\|T_{\bm{A}_{L}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\prod_{s=0}^{L-1}(\|\tilde{\bm{D}}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\tilde{\bm{A}}_{s}\|_{\infty})\leq\nu m^{L}.

Next, we investigate the terms containing
\Delta\bm{D}_{\ell}
. For
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
, define

T_{\bm{D}_{\ell}}:=\big(\bm{A}_{L}\bm{D}_{L-1}\cdots\bm{A}_{\ell+1}\big)\big(\Delta\bm{D}_{\ell}\big)\big(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\cdots\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0}\big).

Its norm can be bounded by

\displaystyle\|T_{\bm{D}_{\ell}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}\prod_{s=\ell+1}^{L-1}(\|{\bm{D}}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|{\bm{A}}_{s}\|_{\infty})\|\Delta{\bm{D}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\|\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\prod_{s=0}^{\ell-1}(\|\tilde{\bm{D}}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\tilde{\bm{A}}_{s}\|_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq Bm^{L-1}\|\Delta{\bm{D}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}.

We now bound
\|\Delta{\bm{D}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}
. Since
\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(z)=m(\max\{z,0\})^{m-1}
, we have

\|\bm{D}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{D}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}=\max_{i}|\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(z_{\ell,i})-\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(\tilde{z}_{\ell,i})|\leq m(m-1)\|\bm{z}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}.

Applying the triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\bm{z}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\big\|(\bm{A}_{\ell}\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell})-(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{A}_{\ell}(\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x}))\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|(\bm{A}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell})\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})+(\bm{b}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{A}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\|\phi_{\ell}(\bm{x})-\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}+\|(\bm{A}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\|(\tilde{\phi}_{\ell}(\bm{x})^{\top},1)^{\top}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 1\cdot\left(t\cdot m\frac{m^{\ell}-1}{m-1}\right)+t\cdot 1=t\cdot\frac{m^{\ell+1}-1}{m-1}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\|T_{{\bm{D}}_{\ell}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq Bm^{L-1}\|\Delta{\bm{D}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq Bm^{L-1}\left(m(m-1)\|\bm{z}_{\ell}-\tilde{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leq Bm^{L-1}\left(m(m-1)\cdot t\frac{m^{\ell+1}-1}{m-1}\right)=tBm^{L}(m^{\ell+1}-1).

Thus, we derive

\begin{split}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\big\|\nabla g(\bm{x})-\nabla\tilde{g}(\bm{x})\big\|_{1}&\leq\nu m^{L}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{L-1}tBm^{L}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{L-1}tBm^{L}(m^{\ell+1}-1)\\
&\leq\nu m^{L}+tLBm^{L}+tBm^{2L+1}.\end{split}

(16)

Combining the results from (
14
) and (
16
), we obtain

\rho_{\infty}(g,\tilde{g})\leq\nu(m^{L}+1)+t\left(Bm\frac{m^{L}-1}{m-1}+LBm^{L}+Bm^{2L+1}\right).

To construct an
u
-cover, we choose
t
and
\nu
such that the right-hand side is at most
u
. This can be done by requiring each component to be at most
u/2
. Setting
\nu(m^{L}+1)=u/2
gives
\nu=u/(2m^{L}+2)
. For the terms involving
t
, it is dominated by
tLBm^{2L}
, which requires

t\asymp\frac{u}{2LKm^{L}}.
From (
15
), the cardinality of the cover
\widetilde{\Phi}
is bounded by

|\widetilde{\Phi}|\leq(2/t+1)^{\sum_{s=0}^{L-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)}(2B/\nu+1)^{d_{L}}.

Plugging the chosen values of
t
and
\nu
, we obtain

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K),\rho_{\infty})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{s=0}^{L-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)\log\left(1+\frac{4LKm^{L}}{u}\right)+d_{L}\log\left(1+\frac{4K(1+m^{-L})}{u}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{s=0}^{L-1}d_{s+1}(d_{s}+1)\log\left(1+\frac{LKm^{L}}{u}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

or any
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{X})
, if
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
satisfies
W\!\geq\!N
,
\mathcal{S}\!\geq\!N(d+1)
, and
K\!\gtrsim\!\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\}
, then
there exists
g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|g(\bm{x})-f(\bm{x})|

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N}

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla(g-f)(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N}.

remark

The proof first establishes the approximation results of

\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K):=\Bigl\{f(\bm{x})=\sum_{i=1}^{W}a_{i}\,\sigma_{m}((\bm{x}^{\top},1)\bm{v}_{i}):\bm{v}_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{d},\sum_{i=1}^{W}|a_{i}|\leq K\Bigr\}

on the unit ball
\mathbb{B}^{d}:=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{x}\|_{2}\leq 1\}
follows the techniques of
[
4
,
56
]
, and then extends the result to the hypercube
\mathcal{X}:=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}
via
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{x}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{d}\}
. Since

\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)\subseteq\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,1,W(d+1),mK(d+1)^{m/2})\subseteq\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(\tilde{W},\tilde{L},\tilde{\mathcal{S}},\tilde{K})
, for any
\tilde{W}\geq W
,
\tilde{L}\geq 1
,
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}\geq W(d+1)
, and
\tilde{K}\geq mK(d+1)^{m/2}
, any approximation result established for
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
directly extends to these larger classes.

The analysis of
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
over
\mathbb{B}^{d}
proceeds by lifting the target function
h\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{d})
to a smooth function
\widetilde{h}
on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
. The extension
\widetilde{h}
is then approximated by a spherical polynomial. Restricting this polynomial back to the unit ball
\mathbb{B}^{d}
yields a function that approximates
h
while preserving key structural properties. Finally, the results of
[
45
]
show that this polynomial approximation can be further approximated by
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
.

In particular, Proposition 3.3 of
[
56
]
shows that for any
h\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{d})
, there exists
\widetilde{h}\in C^{r,\beta}(\mathbb{S}^{d})
with
\alpha=r+\beta
, where
r\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\beta\in(0,1]
, such that

S_{m}\widetilde{h}=h\;\text{ on }\mathbb{B}^{d},\quad\text{ and }\quad\omega_{2s^{*}}(\widetilde{h};t)_{\infty}\leq Ct^{\alpha},

where
S_{m}
is the operator defined in Lemma
A.6
,
s^{*}\in\mathbb{N}
is the smallest integer satisfying
\alpha\leq 2s^{*}
,
\omega_{2s^{*}}(\widetilde{h};t)_{\infty}
denotes the
2s^{*}
-th order modulus of smoothness of
\widetilde{h}
, and
C
is a constant independent of
h
. Moreover,
\widetilde{h}
can be chosen odd (resp. even) when
m
is even (resp. odd).

Next, we approximate
\widetilde{h}
on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d}
using spherical polynomials. This procedure is based on the theory of spherical harmonics, with the key definitions summarized below (see
[
13
]
for details). Let
\mathcal{H}_{\ell}^{d+1}
denote the space of spherical harmonics of degree
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, i.e., the restrictions to
\mathbb{S}^{d}
of real homogeneous harmonic polynomials of degree
\ell
. Its dimension is
a_{\ell}^{d+1}:=\lx@nobreakspace\frac{2\ell+d-1}{\ell}\binom{\ell+d-2}{d-1}
for
\ell\neq 0
, and
a_{0}^{d+1}:=1
. As shown in Theorem 2.2.2 of
[
13
]
,
\mathcal{H}_{\ell}^{d+1}
are mutually orthogonal and their direct sum is dense in
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{d})
, yielding the decomposition
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{d})=\bigoplus_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}_{\ell}^{d+1}
. Then, any
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{d})

can be represented as
f=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\operatorname{proj}_{\ell}f
, where
\operatorname{proj}_{\ell}:L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{d})\mapsto\mathcal{H}_{\ell}^{d+1}
is the orthogonal projection from
L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{d})
onto
\mathcal{H}_{\ell}^{d+1}
, which admits the representation

\operatorname{proj}_{\ell}f(\bm{x})=a_{\ell}^{d+1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d}}f(\bm{y})P_{\ell}(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{y})\,d\tau_{d}(\bm{y}),

where
\tau_{d}
is the normalized surface area measure on
\mathbb{S}^{d}
and
P_{\ell}
is the Gegenbauer polynomial

P_{\ell}(t):=\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{2^{\ell}}\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\Gamma(\ell+d/2)}(1-t^{2})^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\left(\frac{d}{dt}\right)^{\ell}\Big((1-t^{2})^{\ell+\frac{d-2}{2}}\Big),\quad t\in[-1,1],

normalized so that
P_{\ell}(1)=1
. For
\ell\neq 0
,
P_{\ell}
is odd (resp. even) when
\ell
is odd (resp. even).

Let
\Pi_{\ell}(\mathbb{S}^{d})
denote the space of spherical polynomials of degree at most
\ell
. For any
f\in C(\mathbb{S}^{d})
, the error of the best approximation to
f
by
\Pi_{\ell}(\mathbb{S}^{d})
is defined as

E_{\ell}(f)_{\infty}:=\inf_{g\in\Pi_{\ell}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\|f-g\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}.

Since
\Pi_{\ell}(\mathbb{S}^{d})
is finite-dimensional, a best approximation exists but is generally intractable. In practice, near-best approximations are sufficient. To this end, we let
\eta
be a
C^{\infty}
function supported on
[0,2]
with
\eta(t)=1
for
0\leq t\leq 1
, and define

L_{\ell}f(\bm{x}):=f*L_{\ell}(\bm{x})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d}}f(\bm{y})L_{\ell}(\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{y})\,d\tau_{d}(\bm{y}),\quad\bm{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{d},

where
L_{\ell}(t):=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\eta\left(\frac{r}{\ell}\right)a_{r}^{d+1}P_{r}(t)
. Equivalently,

L_{\ell}f(\bm{x})=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\eta\left(\frac{r}{\ell}\right)\operatorname{proj}_{r}f(\bm{x}),\quad\bm{x}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}.

Since
\eta\left(\frac{r}{\ell}\right)=0
for
r\geq 2\ell
,
L_{\ell}f
is a spherical polynomial of degree at most
2\ell-1
. The best approximation error can be characterized in terms of the modulus of smoothness, i.e.,

\|f-L_{\ell}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim E_{\ell}(f)_{\infty}\lesssim\omega_{r}(f;\ell^{-1})_{\infty},

(17)

where the first inequality follows from Theorem 2.6.3 of
[
13
]
and the second from Theorems 4.7.1 and 4.4.2 of
[
13
]
. For
1\leq i\neq j\leq d+1
, define the angular derivative
D_{i,j}=u_{i}\partial_{u_{j}}-u_{j}\partial_{u_{i}}.

Theorem 4.5.5 of
[
13
]
further shows that
L_{\ell}f
also provides a near-best simultaneous approximation to
D_{i,j}f
, in the sense that

\|D_{i,j}f-D_{i,j}L_{\ell}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim E_{\ell}(D_{i,j}f)_{\infty}\lesssim\omega_{r}(D_{i,j}f;\ell^{-1})_{\infty}.

(18)

Now, we define the approximation polynomial
g_{\ell}=L_{\ell}\tilde{h}
. Since
\omega_{2s^{*}}(\widetilde{h};t)_{\infty}\lesssim t^{\alpha}
, and noting from a direct calculation that
S_{m}D_{i,j}\widetilde{h}
consists of polynomial multiples of the derivatives of
h
, (
17
) and (
18
) yield

\|\tilde{h}-g_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim\ell^{-\alpha},\quad\text{and }\;\|D_{i,j}\tilde{h}-D_{i,j}g_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim\ell^{-(\alpha-1)}.

Following
[
56
]
, we consider three smoothness regimes and summarize their main results. In Case I (
\alpha>(d+2m+1)/2
or
\alpha=(d+2m+1)/2
is an even integer), we have
\tilde{h}\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma_{m}}(C(d,m,\alpha))
for some constant
C(d,m,\alpha)
depending on
d,m,
and
\alpha
. Here,
\mathcal{G}_{\sigma_{m}}(K)
is a class of functions defined over
\mathbb{S}^{d}
as follows.

\mathcal{G}_{\sigma_{m}}(K):=\Big\{g\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d}):\;\;g(\bm{u})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{d}}\sigma_{m}(\bm{u}^{\top}\bm{v})d\mu(\bm{v}),\;\|\mu\|\leq K\Big\}.

In Case II (
\alpha=(d+2m+1)/2
is not an even integer), we have
g_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma_{m}}(K)
with
K\lesssim\sqrt{\log\ell}
, leading to

\|\tilde{h}-g_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim\exp\{-\alpha K^{2}\},\quad\text{and }\;\|D_{i,j}(\tilde{h}-g_{\ell})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim\exp\{-(\alpha-1)K^{2}\}.

In Case III (
\alpha<(d+2m+1)/2
), we have
g_{\ell}\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma_{m}}(K)
with
K\lesssim\ell^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2}}
, leading to

\|\tilde{h}-g_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim K^{-\frac{2\alpha}{d+2m+1-2\alpha}},\quad\text{and }\;\|D_{i,j}(\tilde{h}-g_{\ell})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim K^{-\frac{2(\alpha-1)}{d+2m+1-2\alpha}}.

We first study Case III. Define
\phi=S_{m}g_{\ell}
. Proposition 3.3 of
[
56
]
shows that
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(K)
. By Lemma
A.6
, we obtain the following approximation bounds for both the function value and its gradient,

\|h-\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\leq 2^{m/2}\,\|\tilde{h}-g_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim K^{-\frac{2\alpha}{\,d+2m+1-2\alpha}},

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\|\nabla(h-\phi)(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|\tilde{h}-g_{\ell}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\|D_{i,j}(\tilde{h}-g_{\ell})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\lesssim K^{-\frac{2(\alpha-1)}{d+2m+1-2\alpha}}.

Thus,
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(K)
provides a simultaneous approximation of
h
in both function value and gradient. Further, Lemma
A.5
shows that there exists
\phi_{W}\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
such that

\displaystyle\big\|\phi_{W}-\phi\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\lesssim K\cdot W^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2m+1}{2d}},\text{ and }\sup_{\|\bm{s}\|_{1}\leq 1}\big\|D^{\bm{s}}(\phi_{W}-\phi)\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\lesssim K\cdot W^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2m-1}{2d}}.

Combining this with the previous bounds yields

\big\|\phi_{W}-h\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\leq\big\|\phi_{W}-\phi\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}+\big\|\phi-h\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\lesssim K\cdot W^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2m+1}{2d}}+K^{-\frac{2\alpha}{d+2m+1-2\alpha}},

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-h)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-\phi)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}+\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi-h)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim K\cdot W^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2m-1}{2d}}+K^{-\frac{2(\alpha-1)}{d+2m+1-2\alpha}}.

Therefore, with
K\gtrsim W^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}}
, for Case III there exists
\phi_{W}\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
such that

\displaystyle\big\|\phi_{W}-h\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\lesssim W^{-\frac{\alpha}{d}},\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-h)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim W^{-\frac{\alpha-1}{d}}.

For Case II, by a similar argument, there exists
\phi_{W}\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
such that

\displaystyle\big\|\phi_{W}-h\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim K\cdot W^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2m+1}{2d}}+\exp\{-\alpha K^{2}\},\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-h)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim K\cdot W^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{2m-1}{2d}}+\exp\{-(\alpha-1)K^{2}\}.

When
K^{2}\gtrsim\frac{d+2m+1}{2d\alpha}\log W
, this yields

\displaystyle\big\|\phi_{W}-h\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\lesssim W^{-\frac{d+2m+1}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W}\text{ and }\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-h)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim W^{-\frac{d+2m-1}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W}.

For Case I, there exists
\phi_{W}\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,C(d,m,\alpha))
such that

\displaystyle\big\|\phi_{W}-h\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}\lesssim W^{-\frac{d+2m+1}{2d}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-h)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim W^{-\frac{d+2m-1}{2d}}.

Consequently, for all three cases, when
K\gtrsim\max\{W^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log W}\}
, there exists
\phi_{W}\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
such that

\displaystyle\big\|\phi_{W}-h\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim W^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W},\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-h)(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim W^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W}.

(19)

Next, we extend the approximation results from
\mathbb{B}^{d}
to
\mathcal{X}=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|\bm{x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}
. Let
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{X})
be the target function. Define a rescaled function on
\mathbb{B}^{d}
by

\tilde{f}(\bm{y}):=f(\sqrt{d}\bm{y}),\quad\text{ with }\bm{y}=\frac{\bm{x}}{\sqrt{d}}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}.

Then
\tilde{f}
inherits the smoothness of
f
, satisfying
d^{-\alpha/2}\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{d})
. By (
19
), there exists
\phi_{W}\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
of the form

\phi_{W}(\bm{y})=\sum_{i=1}^{W}a_{i}\sigma_{m}((\bm{y}^{\top},1)\bm{v}_{i}),\quad\text{with }\bm{v}_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}\text{ and }\sum_{i=1}^{W}|a_{i}|\leq K,

with
K\gtrsim\max\{W^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log W}\}
, such that

\displaystyle\big\|\phi_{W}-d^{-\alpha/2}\tilde{f}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim W^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W},\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\big\|\nabla(\phi_{W}-d^{-\alpha/2}\tilde{f})(\bm{y})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim W^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W}.

Define the approximation function on
\mathcal{X}
by scaling back as
\tilde{g}(\bm{x}):=\phi_{W}(\bm{x}/\sqrt{d})
. We show that
\tilde{g}
remains within
\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
.
Let
\bm{v}_{i}=(\bm{w}_{i}^{\top},b_{i})^{\top}
with
\bm{w}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
b_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+b_{i}^{2}=1
. Let
\bm{v}^{\prime}_{i}:=(\bm{w}_{i}^{\top}/\sqrt{d},b_{i})^{\top}
and let
c_{i}:=\|\bm{v}^{\prime}_{i}\|_{2}=\sqrt{\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}/d+b_{i}^{2}}
. Since
\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}=1-b_{i}^{2}
and
b_{i}^{2}\in[0,1]
, we have
c_{i}^{2}=(1-b_{i}^{2})/d+b_{i}^{2}\leq 1
. Using the
m
-homogeneity of
\sigma_{m}
and defining the normalized vector
\bm{v}^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\bm{v}^{\prime}_{i}/c_{i}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}
, we can write
\tilde{g}(\bm{x})
as

\tilde{g}(\bm{x})=\sum_{i=1}^{W}a_{i}\sigma_{m}\left(\left(\frac{\bm{x}^{\top}}{\sqrt{d}},1\right)\bm{v}_{i}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{W}(a_{i}c_{i}^{m})\sigma_{m}((\bm{x}^{\top},1)\bm{v}^{\prime\prime}_{i}).

Moreover,
\sum_{i=1}^{W}|a_{i}c_{i}^{m}|\leq\sum_{i=1}^{W}|a_{i}|\cdot 1^{m}\leq K
. Thus,
\tilde{g}\in\mathcal{F}_{\sigma_{m}}(W,K)
. Let
g:=d^{\alpha/2}\tilde{g}.
Then,

\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|g(\bm{x})-f(\bm{x})|\leq\sup_{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}|d^{\alpha/2}\phi_{W}(\bm{y})-\tilde{f}(\bm{y})|\lesssim W^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W}.

(20)

For the gradient, the chain rule yields
\nabla_{\bm{x}}(g-f)(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\nabla_{\bm{y}}(d^{\alpha/2}\phi_{W}-\tilde{f})(\bm{x}/\sqrt{d})
. Taking the supremum norm gives

\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla(g-f)(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{\bm{y}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}\|\nabla(d^{\alpha/2}\phi_{W}-\tilde{f})(\bm{y})\|_{\infty}\lesssim W^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log W}.

(21)

By relabeling
W
in (
20
) and (
21
) as
N
and combining these results with the argument in the first paragraph, we establish that for any
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}(\mathcal{X})
, if
W\geq N
,
\mathcal{S}\geq N(d+1)
, and
K\gtrsim\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\}
, there exists
g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|g(\bm{x})-f(\bm{x})|

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N},\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla(g-f)(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N},

which completes the proof.
∎

uppose Assumption 1 holds with
\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}<\infty
. Assume
m\geq 1
,
W\geq N,\mathcal{S}\geq N(d+1),

K\!\gtrsim\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\},
and
M_{n}\asymp\log n
. Let
\beta=\min\{(d+2m+1)/2,\alpha\}
. Then, for any
\delta>0
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]&\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+N^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}\log N+n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\log n+\delta K\\
&\qquad\;+\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}\log n.\end{split}

If further
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp N\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
and
K\asymp\max\big\{n^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2(d+2\beta)}},\sqrt{\log n}\big\}
, then

\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{2}+\delta K.

remark

Let
f_{1,n}\in\argmin_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)
and
f_{1,\delta}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)
. The excess local worst-case risk can be denoted as

\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\min\big\{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star};\delta),\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,n};\delta)\big\}.

For notational convenience, define

\mathcal{E}_{1,1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star};\delta),\quad\mathcal{E}_{1,2}:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,n};\delta).

Under this notation, the excess local worst-case risk satisfies

\begin{split}{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)=\max\big\{\mathcal{E}_{1,1},\mathcal{E}_{1,2}\big\}.\end{split}

(22)

The subsequent analysis will bound
\mathcal{E}_{1,1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1,2}
separately.

We begin with
\mathcal{E}_{1,1}
. Fix an arbitrary
f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
(to be specified later). Since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(f_{n};\delta)
, we can decompose
\mathcal{E}_{1,1}
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(f_{n};\delta)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{n};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star};\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{n};\delta)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star};\delta).

By Lemma 2.3 in the main text,
0\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)\leq\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}},
which implies

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(f;\delta)-\big\{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(f)\big\}\big|\leq\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}},\text{ and }

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star};\delta)\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star})+\delta\|\ell_{f_{n}}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

Moreover, the Lipschitz constant of the composite loss
\ell(\bm{z};f)
satisfies

\displaystyle\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\leq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}(\|f\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}+1).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1,1}

\displaystyle\leq I_{1,1}+I_{1,2}+3\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}(K+1),

where the errors
I_{1,1}
and
I_{1,2}
are defined by

\displaystyle I_{1,1}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)})+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(f_{n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n}),

\displaystyle I_{1,2}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star}).

Define
H_{n}(f;f_{0}):=\mathbb{E}[\ell(Z;f)-\ell(Z;f_{0})]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{\ell(Z_{i};f)-\ell(Z_{i};f_{0})\}
for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, and define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n}:=\Big\{\big|H_{n}(f;f_{0})\big|\leq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}(\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}+r_{n})\quad\text{ for all }f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\Big\},

where
r_{n}
satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]=r_{n}^{2}/(64M_{n})
with
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}:=\{a(f-f_{0}):a\in[0,1],f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\}
.
Lemma
A.12
shows that there exist constants
b_{1},b_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle P\big\{\mathcal{A}_{n}\big\}\geq 1-2\exp\big\{-b_{1}n\{M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}\}^{2}+b_{2}\log(\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1}))\big\}.

On the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we can bound the two error terms. First,
I_{1,1}
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle I_{1,1}

\displaystyle=H_{n}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};f_{0})-H_{n}(f_{n};f_{0})\leq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}(\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}+\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{2}+2r_{n})

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{3}}{2}\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2},

where
a_{1n}=(2c_{3}^{-1}+c_{2}^{-1})\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}+4\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}
, and the last inequality follows from
2xy\leq a^{-1}x^{2}+ay^{2}
for any
a>0
. Second, Assumption 1 in the main text implies an upper bound on the approximation error
I_{1,2}
as follows.

\displaystyle I_{1,2}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star})\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)\leq c_{1}+c_{2}\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

We now combine these bounds by applying the condition (ii) from Assumption 1 in the main text. Conditioned on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
and assuming
\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}\geq c_{4}
, we have

\begin{split}c_{3}\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}&\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0})\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,\delta}^{\star};\delta)+(d+1)\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\\
&\leq I_{1,1}+I_{1,2}+(3K+d+4)\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\\
&\lesssim c_{1}+2c_{2}\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{3}}{2}\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K.\end{split}

Rearranging terms and choosing
f_{n}
as the best approximation of
f_{0}
in
\mathcal{F}_{n}
leads that

c_{3}\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim c_{1}+c_{2}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K.

This bound holds for the case
\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}\geq c_{4}
. To obtain a general bound valid on
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we must also account for the case where the estimator is within the local region
\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}<c_{4}
, for which the
L^{2}
error is directly bounded by
c_{4}^{2}
. Combining the two cases, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we show

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{1,1}\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+c_{2}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K.\end{split}

Next, we study
\mathcal{E}_{1,2}.
Following a similar analysis and on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}&\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},1}(f_{1,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{1,n};\delta)\\
&\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)})+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(f_{1,n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{1,n})+2\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}(K+1)\\
&\leq c_{2}\|f_{1,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{3}}{2}\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+2\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}(K+1).\end{split}

Consider the case
\min\{\|f_{1,n}-f_{0}\|_{2},\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}\}\geq c_{4}
. Since
f_{1,n}\in\argmin_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)
, Assumption 1 in the main text implies that, for any
f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
,

\displaystyle c_{3}\|f_{1,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{1,n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0})\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}(K+1).

By choosing
f_{n}
as the best approximant to
f_{0}
over
\mathcal{F}_{n}
and applying the previously derived bound for
\|\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}\lesssim c_{1}+c_{2}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K.\end{split}

Furthermore, by considering the complementary cases where one or both terms lie within the local region (where the
L^{2}
error is bounded by
c_{4}^{2}
), we derive the following general bound for
\mathcal{E}_{1,2}
on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
.

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+c_{2}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K.\end{split}

From (
22
), the bound on
{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)
is determined by the bounds on
\mathcal{E}_{1,1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{1,2}
, which implies that under the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
,

\begin{split}{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+c_{2}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K.\end{split}

By Lemma
A.14
and Theorem 3.4 in the main text, for any
N>0
, if

W\geq N,\;\mathcal{S}\geq N(d+1),\;\text{ and }\;K\!\gtrsim\max\big\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\big\},

then the following bounds hold.

r_{n}\lesssim M_{n}n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)},\quad\text{and}\quad\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{d}}\log N.

In this case, there exists positive constant
C
such that
P\{\mathcal{A}_{n}\}\geq 1-C\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}.
Furthermore, it can be shown that
{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\lesssim M_{n}\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.
Therefore,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]&=\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)1_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)1_{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}}\big]\\
&\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+c_{2}\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K+M_{n}\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}P\big\{\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c}\big\}\\
&\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+c_{2}N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{d}}\log N+(c_{2}^{-1}+c_{3}^{-1}+M_{n})\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\\
&\qquad\;+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K+M_{n}\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}.\end{split}

Set
M_{n}\asymp\log n
. To optimize the bound, choose the network architecture and tuning parameters as follows. Let
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp N
and
K\asymp\max\big\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\big\}
. Balancing the errors yields
N\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
, where
\beta=\min\{(d+2m+1)/2,\alpha\}
. Thus,
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
and
K\asymp\max\big\{n^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2(d+2\beta)}},\sqrt{\log n}\big\}
. Substituting these into the bound implies

\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},1}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{2}+\delta K.

Finally, to ensure that the penalty term
\delta K
does not dominate the rate and vanishes asymptotically, we select
\delta
to be sufficiently small. A specific choice
\delta=o(n^{-\frac{d+2m+1+2\beta}{2(d+2\beta)}})
ensures the optimal result. This completes the proof.
∎

uppose Assumption 1 holds with
