\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty},\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}<\infty
. Assume
\alpha>1
,
m\geq 2
,
W\geq N,\mathcal{S}\geq N(d+1),

K\!\gtrsim\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\},
and
M_{n}\asymp\log n
. Let
\beta=\min\{(d+2m+1)/2,\alpha\}
. Then, for any
\delta>0
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)]&\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+N^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}\log N+n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\log n\\
&\qquad+\delta\big\{1+N^{-\frac{\beta-1}{d}}\sqrt{\log N}+\{n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\}^{1/(2k^{\star})}\{Lm^{L}\}^{1/k^{\star}}K\big\}\\
&\qquad+\delta^{\min\{k,2\}}(K^{2}+Lm^{L}K)+\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}\log n.\end{split}

If further
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp N\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
and
K\asymp\max\big\{n^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2(d+2\beta)}},\sqrt{\log n}\big\}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{2}+\delta\{1+n^{-\frac{\beta}{k^{\star}(d+2\beta)}}K\}+\delta^{\min\{k,2\}}K^{2}.

remark

We first consider the case
k\geq 2
, while the analysis for
1<k<2
follows similarly.
Let
f_{k,n}\in\argmin_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)
and
f_{k,\delta}^{\star}\in\argmin_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)
. The excess local worst-case risk can be expressed as

\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\min\big\{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta),\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)\big\}.

For notational convenience, define

\mathcal{E}_{k,1}:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta),\quad\mathcal{E}_{k,2}:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta).

Under this notation, the excess local worst-case risk satisfies

\begin{split}{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)=\max\big\{\mathcal{E}_{k,1},\mathcal{E}_{k,2}\big\}.\end{split}

The subsequent analysis will bound
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{k,2}
separately.

We begin with
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
. Fix an arbitrary
f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
(to be specified later). Since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{n};\delta)
, we can decompose
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k,1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{n};\delta)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\big\{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)\big\}

\displaystyle:=I_{k,1}+I_{k,2}.

To analyze
I_{k,1}
, note that for losses of the form
\ell(\bm{z};f)=\ell(f(\bm{x})-y)
and any differentiable functions
f,\widetilde{f}
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{\bm{z}}\ell(f(\bm{x})-y)-\nabla_{\bm{z}}\ell(\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})-y)\big\|_{1}=\Big\|\ell^{\prime}(f(\bm{x})-y)\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f(\bm{x})\\
-1\end{pmatrix}-\ell^{\prime}(\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})-y)\begin{pmatrix}\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\\
-1\end{pmatrix}\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\big|\ell^{\prime}(f(\bm{x})-y)-\ell^{\prime}(\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})-y)\big|\;\Big\|\begin{pmatrix}\nabla f(\bm{x})\\
-1\end{pmatrix}\Big\|_{1}+|\ell^{\prime}(\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})-y)|\;\|\nabla f(\bm{x})-\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\,|f(\bm{x})-\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})|\;\big(\|\nabla f(\bm{x})\|_{1}+1\big)+\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla f(\bm{x})-\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\|_{1}.

Since
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla f(\bm{x})\|_{1}\leq d
, it follows that for any
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{\bm{z}}\ell(f(\bm{x})-y)-\nabla_{\bm{z}}\ell(\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})-y)\big\|_{1}\leq C_{0}\big\{|f(\bm{x})-\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})|+\|\nabla f(\bm{x})-\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\|_{1}\big\},

(23)

where
C_{0}=\max\{(d+1)\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}},\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\}
.
By a similar argument and the bound
\|\nabla f(\bm{x})-\nabla f(\tilde{\bm{x}})\|_{1}\leq d\sup_{\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla^{2}f(\bm{x}^{\prime})\|_{\infty}\|\bm{x}-\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{\infty}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla_{\bm{z}}\ell(f(\bm{x})-y)-\nabla_{\bm{z}}\ell(f(\tilde{\bm{x}})-\tilde{y})\big\|_{1}\leq\mathcal{D}(f)(\|\bm{x}-\tilde{\bm{x}}\|_{\infty}+|y-\tilde{y}|),

where
\mathcal{D}(f)=\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}(\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla f(\bm{x})\|_{1}+1)^{2}+d\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla^{2}f(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}
. Define

\mathcal{V}(f)=\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla\ell(Z;f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big],\qquad\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\nabla\ell(Z_{i};f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}.

By Lemma 2.5 in the main text,

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)-\delta\mathcal{V}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(f)-\delta\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f).

(24)

Moreover, Lemma
A.4
ensures uniform boundedness

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}(f)\lesssim d^{2},\quad\text{ and }\quad\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)\lesssim K^{2}+Lm^{L}K.

(25)

Combining (
24
) with Lemma
A.15
, and noting that
1/k^{\star}<1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f;\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(f)+\delta\big\{\mathcal{V}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}\big\}+2\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f)

\displaystyle\leq H_{n}(f;f_{0})+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}(f_{0})+\delta\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}+2\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f),

where
H_{n}(f;f_{0}):=\mathbb{E}[\ell(Z;f)-\ell(Z;f_{0})]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\ell(Z_{i};f)-\ell(Z_{i};f_{0})\big\}.

Define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{n}:=\Big\{\big|H_{n}(f;f_{0})\big|\leq 2\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}(\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}+r_{n})\;\text{ for all }f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\Big\},

where
r_{n}
satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]=r_{n}^{2}/(64M_{n})
with
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}:=\{a(f-f_{0}):a\in[0,1],f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\}
. Lemma
A.12
shows that there exist constants
b_{1},b_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle P\big\{\mathcal{A}_{n}\big\}\geq 1-2\exp\big\{-b_{1}n\{M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}\}^{2}+b_{2}\log(\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1}))\big\}.

Consequently, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{k,1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)

\displaystyle\leq H_{n}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};f_{0})-H_{n}(f_{n};f_{0})+2\delta\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}+4\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}(\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}+\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{2}+2r_{n})

\displaystyle\qquad+2\delta\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}+4\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f).

(26)

To bound
|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)|^{1/k^{\star}}
, we define
g_{f}(\bm{z}):=\|\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}
.
By symmetrization argument, it follows

\mathbb{E}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|=\mathbb{E}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\Big|\mathcal{\mathbb{}}E[g_{f}(Z)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{f}(Z_{i})\Big|\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g_{f}(Z_{i})\Big|,

where
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are i.i.d. Rademacher variables independent of the data. By (
23
) and the mean-value theorem on the map
t\mapsto t^{k^{\star}}
, for any
f,\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\big|g_{f}(\bm{z})-g_{\widetilde{f}}(\bm{z})\big|

\displaystyle\leq k^{\star}\sup_{\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\|_{1}^{\,k^{\star}-1}\cdot\big\|\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)-\nabla\ell(\bm{z};\widetilde{f})\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq k^{\star}C_{1}^{\,k^{\star}-1}C_{0}\big\{|f(\bm{x})-\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})|+\|\nabla f(\bm{x})-\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\|_{1}\big\},

where
C_{1}:=\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}(\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\|\nabla f(\bm{x})\|_{1}+1)\leq\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}(K+1).

Let
\mathcal{F}_{n}
be equipped with the metric
\rho_{\infty}(f,\widetilde{f})=\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}\big\{|f(\bm{x})-\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})|+\|\nabla f(\bm{x})-\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\|_{1}\big\}.
Then it follows

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}}\big|g_{f}(\bm{z})-g_{\widetilde{f}}(\bm{z})\big|\leq k^{\star}C_{1}^{\,k^{\star}-1}C_{0}\rho_{\infty}(f,\widetilde{f}).

According to Theorem 3.3 in the main text,

\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K),\rho_{\infty})\lesssim\mathcal{S}\log\left(1+LKm^{L}u^{-1}\right).

Applying Lemma
A.8
and noting that
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|g_{f}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}^{k^{*}}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g_{f}(Z_{i})\Big|&\leq\inf_{\eta\geq 0}\bigg\{4\eta+12\int_{\eta}^{C_{1}^{k^{*}}}\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}\big(u/(k^{\star}C_{1}^{\,k^{\star}-1}C_{0}),\mathcal{F}_{n},\rho_{\infty}\big)}{n}}du\bigg\}\\
&\lesssim\inf_{\eta\geq 0}\bigg\{4\eta+12\int_{\eta}^{C_{1}^{k^{*}}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathcal{S}\log\big(1+LKm^{L}k^{\star}C_{1}^{\,k^{\star}-1}C_{0}u^{-1}\big)}{n}}du\bigg\}\\
&\lesssim n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}}\int_{0}^{C_{1}^{k^{*}}}\sqrt{\log\big(1+LKm^{L}k^{\star}C_{1}^{\,k^{\star}-1}C_{0}u^{-1}\big)}du\\
&\lesssim n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}}LKm^{L}k^{\star}C_{1}^{k^{*}-1}C_{0}.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{1/k^{\star}}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|\big]^{1/k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{1/k^{\star}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g_{f}(Z_{i})\big|\Big\}^{1/k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-1/(2k^{\star})}\mathcal{S}^{1/(2k^{\star})}\{LKm^{L}k^{\star}C_{1}^{k^{*}-1}\}^{1/k^{\star}}.

(27)

Next, we investigate the approximation error term
I_{k,2}
. Applying (
24
) together with the triangle inequality for the norm yields

\displaystyle I_{k,2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star})+\delta\big\{\mathcal{V}(f_{n})^{1/k^{\star}}-\mathcal{V}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star})^{1/k^{\star}}\big\}+\delta^{2}\big\{\mathcal{D}(f_{n})+\mathcal{D}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star})\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)+\delta\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\|\nabla\ell(Z;f_{n})-\nabla\ell(Z;f_{0})\|_{1}\big|^{k^{\star}}\Big]^{1/k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\quad+\delta\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\|\nabla\ell(Z;f_{0})-\nabla\ell(Z;f_{k,\delta}^{\star})\|_{1}\big|^{k^{\star}}\Big]^{1/k^{\star}}+\delta^{2}\big\{\mathcal{D}(f_{n})+\mathcal{D}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star})\big\}.

By (
23
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\|\nabla\ell(Z;f_{n})-\nabla\ell(Z;f_{0})\|_{1}\big|^{k^{\star}}\Big]^{1/k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\;\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[|f_{n}(X)-f_{0}(X)|^{k^{\star}}\big]^{1/k^{\star}}+\mathbb{E}\big[\|\nabla(f_{n}-f_{0})(X)\|_{1}^{k^{\star}}\big]^{1/k^{\star}}\Big\}.

Moreover, it holds that
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\sup_{\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}}\|\nabla\ell(\bm{z};f)\|_{1}\leq\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}(d+1).

Therefore, we show

\displaystyle I_{k,2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{n})-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f)+\delta\,C_{0}\big\{\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{k^{*}}+\big\|\|\nabla(f_{n}-f_{0})\|_{1}\big\|_{k^{\star}}\big\}

\displaystyle\qquad+4\delta d\,\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}+\delta^{2}\big\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}(f)\big\}.

(28)

Combining (
26
) and (
28
), and under Assumption 1 in the main text, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k,1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)\leq I_{k,1}+I_{k,2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}+\frac{c_{3}}{2}\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+2c_{2}\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\delta C_{0}\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{k^{\star}}+\delta C_{0}\big\|\|\nabla(f_{n}-f_{0})\|_{1}\big\|_{k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\qquad\;+2\delta\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}+4\delta d\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}+5\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)

\displaystyle\qquad\;+\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}(f)+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2},

where
a_{1n}=(2c_{3}^{-1}+c_{2}^{-1})\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}^{2}+4\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}M_{n}.

Assuming
\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}\geq c_{4}
, condition (ii) of Assumption 1 in the main text implies that

\displaystyle c_{3}\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{0};\delta)+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\{\delta\mathcal{V}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}+\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f)\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}_{k,1}+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\{\delta\mathcal{V}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}+\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f)\}.

Substituting the bound for
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
into this inequality leads to the following upper bound.

\displaystyle\frac{c_{3}}{2}\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}+2c_{2}\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\delta C_{0}\|f_{n}-f_{0}\|_{k^{\star}}+\delta C_{0}\big\|\|\nabla(f_{n}-f_{0})\|_{1}\big\|_{k^{\star}}

\displaystyle\qquad\;+2\delta\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}+6\delta d\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}+5\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)

\displaystyle\qquad\;+2\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}(f)+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}.

In the complementary case,
\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}<c_{4}
, its
L^{2}
error is then bounded by
c_{4}^{2}
. Combining these results, choosing
f_{n}
as the best approximation of
f_{0}
in
\mathcal{F}_{n}
, and incorporating the
c_{4}^{2}
term for the local case, we establish that on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{k,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\Big\{c_{2}\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\delta C_{0}\|f-f_{0}\|_{k^{\star}}+\delta C_{0}\big\|\|\nabla(f-f_{0})\|_{1}\big\|_{k^{\star}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\;+\delta\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}+\delta d\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}+\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)

\displaystyle\qquad\;+\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}(f)+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}.

Next, we analyze
\mathcal{E}_{k,2}
. Following a similar decomposition as for
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
and applying (
24
), we obtain on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
,

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{k,2}&=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)\\
&\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)\\
&\leq 2\|\ell^{\prime}\|_{\infty}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}(\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}+\|f_{k,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}+2r_{n})\\
&\qquad+2\delta\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{1/k^{\star}}+4\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f).\end{split}

(29)

The term
\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}
has already been examined in the analysis of
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
. To bound
\|f_{k,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}
, we first assume
\|f_{k,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}\geq c_{4}
. Since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)
for any
f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, Assumption 1 in the main text implies that

\displaystyle c_{3}\|f_{k,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{k,n})-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}}(f_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{0};\delta)+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\{\delta\mathcal{V}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}+\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f)\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\{\delta\mathcal{V}(f)^{1/k^{\star}}+\delta^{2}\mathcal{D}(f)\}.

Choosing
f_{n}
as the best approximation of
f_{0}
in
\mathcal{F}_{n}
and bounding
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)
similar to
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
yields an upper bound on
c_{3}\|f_{k,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}
in this non-local regime. In the complementary case where
\|f_{k,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}<c_{4}
, its
L^{2}
error is directly bounded by
c_{4}^{2}
. Substituting the bounds for
\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}
and
\|f_{k,n}-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}
into (
29
) and applying Young’s inequality show that
\mathcal{E}_{k,2}
satisfies the same upper bound as that derived for
\mathcal{E}_{k,1}
on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
. Consequently, on the event
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we obtain

\begin{split}{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)&=\max\big\{\mathcal{E}_{k,1},\mathcal{E}_{k,2}\big\}\\
&\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\Big\{c_{2}\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\delta C_{0}\|f-f_{0}\|_{k^{\star}}+\delta C_{0}\big\|\|\nabla(f-f_{0})\|_{1}\big\|_{k^{\star}}\Big\}\\
&\qquad\;+\delta\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}+\delta d\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}+\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)\\
&\qquad\;+\delta^{2}\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}(f)+a_{1n}M_{n}^{-2}r_{n}^{2}.\end{split}

From Lemma
A.14
, we obtain
r_{n}\lesssim M_{n}n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)}
, and there exists constant
C
such that
P\{\mathcal{A}_{n}\}\geq 1-C\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}.

Moreover,
{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\leq 2M_{n}\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}
. This is because

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widetilde{f};\delta)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\ell(f(X)-Y)]-\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\ell(\widetilde{f}(X)-Y)]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\big|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\ell(f(X)-Y)-\ell(\widetilde{f}(X)-Y)]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}\|f-\widetilde{f}\|_{\infty},

which implies that
\mathcal{E}_{k,1},\mathcal{E}_{k,2}\leq 2M_{n}\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}
. Therefore, we show

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)]&=\mathbb{E}[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)1_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}]+\mathbb{E}[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)1_{\mathcal{A}^{c}_{n}}]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)1_{\mathcal{A}_{n}}]+2M_{n}\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}P(\mathcal{A}_{n}^{c})\\
&\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\Big\{c_{2}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\delta C_{0}\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}+\delta C_{0}\big\|\|\nabla(f-f_{0})\|_{1}\big\|_{\infty}\Big\}\\
&\qquad\;+(c_{2}^{-1}+c_{3}^{-1}+M_{n})\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}^{2}n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)+\delta\mathbb{E}[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{V}(f)-\mathcal{V}_{n}(f)\big|^{\,1/k^{\star}}]\\
&\qquad\;+\delta\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}+\delta^{2}\big\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{D}(f)+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{D}(f)\big\}+M_{n}\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}.\end{split}

Theorem 3.4 in the main text shows that if
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
satisfies
W\!\geq\!N
,
\mathcal{S}\!\geq\!N(d+1)
, and
K\gtrsim\!\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\}
, then
there exists
g\in\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
such that

\displaystyle\|g-f_{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N},

\displaystyle\|\|\nabla(g-f_{0})\|_{1}\|_{\infty}\lesssim dN^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N}.

Consequently, we derive

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)]&\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+c_{2}N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{d}}\log N+(c_{2}^{-1}+c_{3}^{-1}+M_{n})\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}^{2}n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\\
&\qquad\;+\delta\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}+\delta C_{0}N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N}\\
&\qquad\;+\delta\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}\{n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\}^{1/(2k^{\star})}\{Lm^{L}\}^{1/k^{\star}}K\\
&\qquad\;+\delta^{2}(\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}K^{2}+\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}Lm^{L}K)+M_{n}\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}.\end{split}

To achieve the optimal rate, we set

\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp N,\;K\asymp\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\},\;\text{and}\;N\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}},

where
\beta=\min\{(d+2m+1)/2,\alpha\}
. Thus,
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
and
K\asymp\max\big\{n^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2(d+2\beta)}},\sqrt{\log n}\big\}
. Substituting these into the bound implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{2}+\delta\{1+n^{-\frac{\beta}{k^{\star}(d+2\beta)}}K\}+\delta^{2}K^{2}.

To ensure that the terms involving
\delta
and
\delta^{2}
do not dominate the statistical rate and vanish asymptotically, we choose
\delta
sufficiently small. In particular, setting

\delta=o\big(n^{-\frac{\max\{4\beta,2k^{-1}\beta+d+2m+1\}}{2(d+2\beta)}}\big)

guarantees the optimal convergence rate.

For the case
1<k<2
, the main difference is that the
\delta^{2}
term in (
24
) is replaced by
\delta^{k}
, while the rest of the analysis remains similar. The proof is therefore complete.

∎

ssume
\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}<\infty
. For any
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
satisfying
W_{k}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

Moreover, under the conditions of Theorem 4.1 for
k=1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(\widehat{f}_{1,\delta}^{(n)})\big]\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{2}+\delta K.

Alternatively, under the conditions of Theorem 4.3 for
k>1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)})\big]\lesssim c_{1}+c_{4}^{2}+n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{2}+\delta\{1+n^{-\frac{\beta}{k^{\star}(d+2\beta)}}K\}+\delta^{\min\{k,2\}}K^{2}.

remark

For any
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
such that
W_{1}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta
, the triangle inequality for the Wasserstein distance implies that the ambiguity set centered at
\mathbb{P}
is contained within a larger set centered at
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
, that is,

\displaystyle\big\{\mathbb{Q}:W_{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta\big\}\subseteq\big\{\mathbb{Q}:W_{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})\leq 2\delta\big\}.

Lemma 2.3 in the main text then implies

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},1}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)\leq\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}^{\prime})\leq 2\delta}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{Q}}(f)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)\leq 2\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

Moreover, noting that
W_{1}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})\leq W_{k}(\mathbb{P},\mathbb{Q})
for any
1\leq k\leq\infty
, we have

0\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)\leq 2\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

Taking the infimum over
f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}
on both sides yields

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)\leq\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\big\{\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)+2\delta\|\ell_{f}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\big\}\leq\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)+2(d+1)\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.

Therefore, we show

\begin{split}{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)&\leq\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)+2(d+1)\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)+\delta\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}.\end{split}

This result, together with Theorems 4.1 and 4.3 completes the proof.
∎

uppose Assumption 2 holds. Assume
\alpha\!>\!1
,
m\!\geq\!2
,
W\!\geq\!N,\mathcal{S}\!\geq\!N(d+1),

K\!\gtrsim\!\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\},
and
M_{n}\asymp\log n
. Let
\beta=\min\{(d+2m+1)/2,\alpha\}
. Then, for any
\delta>0
and
k\geq 2
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]&\lesssim N^{-\frac{2\beta}{d}}\log N+n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\{\log n\}^{3}+n^{-1}\log n\\
&\qquad+\delta\big\{1+N^{-\frac{\beta-1}{d}}\sqrt{\log N}+\{n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\}^{1/(2k^{\star})}\{Lm^{L}\}^{1/k^{\star}}K\big\}\log n\\
&\qquad+\delta^{2}\big\{1+K^{2}+Lm^{L}K\big\}\log n.\end{split}

If further
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp N\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
and
K\asymp\max\big\{n^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2(d+2\beta)}},\sqrt{\log n}\big\}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]\lesssim n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{4}+\delta\big\{1+n^{-\frac{\beta}{k^{\star}(d+2\beta)}}K\big\}\log n+\delta^{2}K^{2}\log n.

In particular, if
\delta=o\big(n^{-\frac{c}{2(d+2\beta)}}\big)
, with
c=\max\{4\beta,2k^{-1}\beta+d+2m+1\}
, then

\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]\lesssim n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{4}.

remark

Let
\beta_{n}\geq M_{n}\geq 1
be a sequence of positive numbers, where
M_{n}
denotes the truncation level associated with
\mathcal{F}_{n}
. We define the truncated loss function
\ell_{n}(u,y):=(u-T_{\beta_{n}}y)^{2}
, where
T_{\beta_{n}}
is the truncation operator at level
\beta_{n}
. The corresponding local worst-case risks are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta):=\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell_{n}(f(X),Y)\right]\;\text{ and }\;\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f;\delta):=\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}_{n})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell_{n}(f(X),Y)\right].

With a slight abuse of notation, let
\ell(u,y)=(u-y)^{2}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)=\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell(f(X),Y)\right]\;\text{ and }\;\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f;\delta)=\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}_{n})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell(f(X),Y)\right].

Under Assumption 2 in the main text, we can establish upper bounds for
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)|
and
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f;\delta)|.
Specifically, for any
\bm{z}^{\prime}=(\bm{x}^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Z}
,

\displaystyle\big|\{f(\bm{x}^{\prime})-y^{\prime}\}^{2}-\{f(\bm{x}^{\prime})-T_{\beta_{n}}y^{\prime}\}^{2}\big|\leq 2\big|f(\bm{x}^{\prime})-T_{\beta_{n}}y^{\prime}\big|\big|y^{\prime}-T_{\beta_{n}}y^{\prime}\big|+\big|y^{\prime}-T_{\beta_{n}}y^{\prime}\big|^{2}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\big|y^{\prime}-T_{\beta_{n}}y^{\prime}\big|\leq\big|y^{\prime}-y\big|+\big|y-T_{\beta_{n}}y\big|+\big|T_{\beta_{n}}y-T_{\beta_{n}}y^{\prime}\big|\leq 2\big|y-y^{\prime}\big|+\big|y-T_{\beta_{n}}y\big|,

which further implies

\displaystyle\big|y^{\prime}-T_{\beta_{n}}y^{\prime}\big|^{2}\leq 8\big|y-y^{\prime}\big|^{2}+2\big|y-T_{\beta_{n}}y\big|^{2}.

Let
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
be any distribution within
\{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta\}
. There exist coupled random variables
(X,Y)\sim\mathbb{P}
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\sim\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{E}[\max\{\|X^{\prime}-X\|_{\infty},|Y^{\prime}-Y|\}^{k}]^{1/k}=W_{k}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\mathbb{P}).
Setting
\Delta=Y^{\prime}-Y
, we have
\mathbb{E}[|\Delta|^{2}]\leq\mathbb{E}[|\Delta|^{k}]^{2/k}\leq\delta^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}[|\Delta|]\leq\delta
. Combining the above results, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}\big[\big|\ell(f(X^{\prime}),Y^{\prime})-\ell_{n}(f(X^{\prime}),Y^{\prime})\big|\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}\big[2(M_{n}+\beta_{n})\big|Y^{\prime}-T_{\beta_{n}}Y^{\prime}\big|+\big|Y^{\prime}-T_{\beta_{n}}Y^{\prime}\big|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[2(M_{n}+\beta_{n})\big(2|\Delta|+\big|Y-T_{\beta_{n}}Y\big|\big)\big]+\mathbb{E}\big[8|\Delta|^{2}+2\big|Y-T_{\beta_{n}}Y\big|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq 8\beta_{n}\delta+8\delta^{2}+4\beta_{n}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[|Y|1\{|Y|>\beta_{n}\}]+2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[|Y|^{2}1\{|Y|>\beta_{n}\}].

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[|Y|^{2}1\{|Y|>\beta_{n}\}\big]+4\beta_{n}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[|Y|1\{|Y|>\beta_{n}\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{16}{\sigma_{Y}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}^{2}}{8}|Y|^{2}\exp\big\{\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}(|Y|-\beta_{n})\big\}\big]+\tfrac{8\beta_{n}}{\sigma_{Y}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}|Y|\exp\big\{\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}(|Y|-\beta_{n})\big\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{16}{\sigma_{Y}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\exp\{\sigma_{Y}|Y|\}\exp\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\}\big]+\tfrac{8\beta_{n}}{\sigma_{Y}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\exp\{\sigma_{Y}|Y|\}\exp\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\big(\tfrac{16}{\sigma_{Y}^{2}}+\tfrac{8\beta_{n}}{\sigma_{Y}}\big)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\exp\{\sigma_{Y}|Y|\}\big]\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\big(\tfrac{16}{\sigma_{Y}^{2}}+\tfrac{8}{\sigma_{Y}}\big)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\exp\{\sigma_{Y}|Y|\}\big]\Big\}\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\},

where the first inequality follows from
\exp\{\sigma_{Y}(|Y|-\beta_{n})/2\}\geq 1
when
|Y|>\beta_{n}
, and the second inequality follows from
\exp\{x\}\geq\max\{x^{2}/2,x\}
for any
x\geq 0.
Hence,

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}\big[\big|\ell(f(X^{\prime}),Y^{\prime})-\ell_{n}(f(X^{\prime}),Y^{\prime})\big|\big]\leq 8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\},\end{split}

(30)

where
C=16(\sigma_{Y}^{-2}+\sigma_{Y}^{-1})\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\exp\{\sigma_{Y}|Y|\}]
.
Since (
30
) holds for any distribution in the set
\{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta\}
, for any uniformly bounded function class
\mathcal{G}_{n}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{G}_{n})&:=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)\big|\\
&=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big|\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell(f(X),Y)\right]-\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell_{n}(f(X),Y)\right]\big|\\
&\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{G}_{n}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\big|\ell(f(X),Y)-\ell_{n}(f(X),Y)\big|\right]\\
&\leq 8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}.\end{split}

(31)

Similarly,

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P}_{n};\mathcal{G}_{n})

\displaystyle:=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)\big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{G}_{n}}\big|\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}_{n})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell(f(X),Y)\right]-\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}_{n})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell_{n}(f(X),Y)\right]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{G}_{n}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}:W_{k}(\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{P}_{n})\leq\delta}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\big|\ell(f(X),Y)-\ell_{n}(f(X),Y)\big|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+4\beta_{n}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}[|Y|1\{|Y|>\beta_{n}\}]+2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}_{n}}[|Y|^{2}1\{|Y|>\beta_{n}\}].

By taking the expectation on both sides, we show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P}_{n};\mathcal{G}_{n})]\leq 8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}.

(32)

Using the bounds in (
31
) and (
32
), we now show that the analysis of
{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)
can be reduced to studying the truncated loss
\ell_{n}
. Following the notation in the proof of Theorem 4.3, we decompose

\begin{split}{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\leq\max\big\{I_{k,1}+I_{k,2},I_{k,3}\big\},\end{split}

where
I_{k,1}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta),

I_{k,2}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)
, and
I_{k,3}=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)
. We define their truncated analogues
I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}
,
I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}}
, and
I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}
by replacing
\ell
with the truncated loss
\ell_{n}
. Specifically,

\displaystyle I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{n};\delta),

\displaystyle I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,\delta}^{\beta_{n}};\delta),

\displaystyle I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)+\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,n};\delta),

where
f_{k,\delta}^{\beta_{n}}\in\argmin_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)
. For any
f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|I_{k,1}-I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big|+\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{n};\delta)\big|+\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{n};\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})+2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P}_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\big|I_{k,2}-I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{n};\delta)\big|+\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,\delta}^{\beta_{n}};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})+\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}),

where the last inequality follows from

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,\delta}^{\beta_{n}};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,\delta}^{\star};\delta)\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)|=\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}).

A similar argument yields that for any
f_{n}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\big|I_{k,3}-I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big|+\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\qquad+\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}_{n},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,n};\delta)\big|+\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,n};\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})+2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P}_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}).

Moreover, for the truncated risk,
f_{k,n}
satisfies
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,n};\delta)\leq\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)+2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})
. This is because

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f_{k,n};\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f_{k,n};\delta)\big|+\big|\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})+\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)|\leq 2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n}).

Combining the above bounds, we obtain

\begin{split}{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)&\leq\max\big\{I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}+I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}}+\big|I_{k,1}-I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}+I_{k,2}-I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}}\big|,I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}+\big|I_{k,3}-I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}\big|\big\}\\
&\leq\max\big\{I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}+I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}},I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}\big\}+3\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})+2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P}_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n})+\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}).\end{split}

Therefore, it suffices to analyze
I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}
,
I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}}
, and
I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}
, which are associated with the truncated loss
\ell_{n}
. The truncation ensures
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}|f(\bm{x})-T_{\beta_{n}}y|\leq 2\beta_{n}
, implying
\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\|_{\infty}\leq 4\beta_{n}
and
\|\ell^{{}^{\prime}}_{n}\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}=2.
Let
f_{0}^{\beta_{n}}(\bm{x}):=\mathbb{E}[T_{\beta_{n}}Y|X=\bm{x}]
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\{f(X)-T_{\beta_{n}}Y\}^{2}\big]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}\big[\{f_{0}^{\beta_{n}}(X)-T_{\beta_{n}}Y\}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=\|f-f_{0}^{\beta_{n}}\|_{2}^{2},

which verifies Assumption 1 in the main text for the truncated loss with
c_{1}=c_{4}=0
and
c_{2}=c_{3}=1
. By arguments similar to those in the proof of Theorem 4.3, if
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{N}(W,L,\mathcal{S},K)
satisfies
W\!\geq\!N
,
\mathcal{S}\!\geq\!N(d+1)
, and
K\gtrsim\max\{N^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2d}},\sqrt{\log N}\}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\max\big\{I_{k,1}^{\beta_{n}}+I_{k,2}^{\beta_{n}},I_{k,3}^{\beta_{n}}\big\}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{d}}\log N+M_{n}\beta_{n}^{2}n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)+\delta\beta_{n}

\displaystyle\qquad\;+\delta\beta_{n}N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N}+\delta\beta_{n}\{n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\}^{1/(2k^{\star})}\{Lm^{L}\}^{1/k^{\star}}K

\displaystyle\qquad\;+\delta^{2}(K^{2}+Lm^{L}K)\beta_{n}+M_{n}\beta_{n}\exp\{-\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\}+\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})].

Setting
\max\{2\log n/\sigma_{Y},M_{n}\}\leq\beta_{n}\leq c\log n
for some positive constant
c
and letting
M_{n}\asymp\log n
, (
31
) and (
32
) imply

\mathbb{E}\big[4\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{F}_{n})+2\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P}_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n})+\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{H}^{\alpha})\big]\lesssim(\delta+\delta^{2})\log n+n^{-1}\log n.

Consequently, we derive

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]&\lesssim N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m+1,2\alpha\}}{d}}\log N+n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)\{\log n\}^{3}+n^{-1}\log n\\
&+\delta\big\{1+N^{-\frac{\min\{d+2m-1,2(\alpha-1)\}}{2d}}\sqrt{\log N}+\{n^{-1}\mathcal{S}\}^{1/(2k^{\star})}\{Lm^{L}\}^{1/k^{\star}}K\big\}\log n\\
&+\delta^{2}\{1+K^{2}+Lm^{L}K\}\log n.\end{split}

To achieve the optimal rate, let
\beta=\min\{(d+2m+1)/2,\alpha\}
, and set
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp N\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
and
K\asymp\max\big\{n^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2(d+2\beta)}},\sqrt{\log n}\big\}
. Substituting these into the above bound implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[{\cal E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{4}+\delta\{1+n^{-\frac{\beta}{k^{\star}(d+2\beta)}}K\}\log n+\delta^{2}K^{2}\log n.

To ensure that the terms involving
\delta
and
\delta^{2}
do not dominate the statistical rate, we choose
\delta
sufficiently small. In particular, setting
\delta=o\big(n^{-\frac{\max\{4\beta,2k^{-1}\beta+d+2m+1\}}{2(d+2\beta)}}\big)
ensures the optimal convergence rate. This completes the proof.
∎

uppose the conditions of Theorem 5.1 hold and
\delta=o\big(n^{-\frac{c}{2(d+2\beta)}}\big)
, with
c=\max\{4\beta,2k^{-1}\beta+d+2m+1\}
. For any
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
satisfying
W_{k}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta
, and assuming
f_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{X})}^{2}\big]\lesssim n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{4}.

remark

From the proof of Theorem 5.1, we have established that

\displaystyle\big|\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)-\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)|=\Delta_{k}(\mathbb{P};\mathcal{H}^{\alpha})

\displaystyle\leq 8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\},

where
C=16(\sigma_{Y}^{-2}+\sigma_{Y}^{-1})\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}}[\exp\{\sigma_{Y}|Y|\}]
. For any distribution
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
such that
W_{k}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime},\mathbb{P})\leq\delta
and
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})\sim\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
, define the truncated natural risk
\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f):=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}[\ell_{n}(f(X^{\prime}),Y^{\prime})]
. We can bound the difference between the original and truncated risks under
\mathbb{P}^{\prime}
as follows.

\displaystyle\big|\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}|\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)-\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)\big|

\displaystyle\leq 8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}.

From the proof of Theorem 4.5, for the truncated loss we also have

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)\leq\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}^{\beta_{n}}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)+8(d+1)\beta_{n}\delta.

Combining these inequalities yields

\displaystyle-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)

\displaystyle\leq-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}^{\beta_{n}}(f)+8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}

\displaystyle\leq-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}^{\beta_{n}}(f;\delta)+8\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}+8(d+1)\beta_{n}\delta

\displaystyle\leq-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P},k}(f;\delta)+16\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+2C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}+8(d+1)\beta_{n}\delta.

Let
f_{0}^{\prime}(\bm{x}):=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}[Y^{\prime}|X^{\prime}=\bm{x}]
. The risk
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)
decomposes as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}[|Y^{\prime}-f_{0}^{\prime}(X^{\prime})+f_{0}^{\prime}(X^{\prime})-f(X^{\prime})|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}[|Y^{\prime}-f_{0}^{\prime}(X^{\prime})|^{2}+2\{Y^{\prime}-f_{0}^{\prime}(X^{\prime})\}\{f_{0}^{\prime}(X^{\prime})-f(X^{\prime})\}+|f_{0}^{\prime}(X^{\prime})-f(X^{\prime})|^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}[|Y^{\prime}-f_{0}^{\prime}(X^{\prime})|^{2}]+\|f_{0}^{\prime}-f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{X})}^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{X})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)})-\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}^{\alpha}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathbb{P}^{\prime}}(f)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{\mathbb{P},k}(\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)};\delta)]+16\beta_{n}(\delta+\delta^{2})+2C\beta_{n}\exp\big\{-\tfrac{\sigma_{Y}}{2}\beta_{n}\big\}+8(d+1)\beta_{n}\delta.

Setting
\max\{2\log n/\sigma_{Y},M_{n}\}\leq\beta_{n}\leq c\log n
for some positive constant
c
with
M_{n}\asymp\log n
, and combining with Theorem 5.1, we obtain the convergence rate. Specifically, let
\mathcal{S}\asymp W\asymp N\asymp n^{\frac{d}{d+2\beta}}
,
K\asymp\max\big\{n^{\frac{d+2m+1-2\alpha}{2(d+2\beta)}},\sqrt{\log n}\big\}
, and
\delta=o\big(n^{-\frac{\max\{4\beta,2k^{-1}\beta+d+2m+1\}}{2(d+2\beta)}}\big)
, where
\beta=\min\{(d+2m+1)/2,\alpha\}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\widehat{f}_{k,\delta}^{(n)}-f_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{\prime}_{X})}^{2}\lesssim n^{-\frac{2\beta}{d+2\beta}}\{\log n\}^{4}.

This completes the proof.
∎

D
Proof of auxiliary lemmas

Let
\bm{w}_{i}=(w_{i1},\dots,w_{in})^{\top}
denote the
i
-th row of
\bm{W}
. The infinity matrix norm can be denoted by

\|\bm{W}\|_{\infty}=\max_{1\leq i\leq m}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left|w_{ij}\right|=\max_{1\leq i\leq m}\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{1}.

According to Lemma
A.1
, there exists a set

\big\{\bm{u}_{j}:j=1,\dots,(2r/u+1)^{n}\big\}

satisfying
\|\bm{u}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for
j=1,\dots,(2r/u+1)^{n}
such that for any
\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying
\|\bm{w}\|_{1}\leq r
, one has

\big\|\bm{w}-\tilde{\bm{w}}\big\|_{1}\leq u,\quad\tilde{\bm{w}}=r\bm{u}_{j},

for some
j\in\{1,\dots,(2r/u+1)^{n}\}
. For each
\bm{w}_{i}
, let
\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{i}
denote the associated approximation constructed above, and let
\widetilde{\bm{W}}
denote the matrix with
i
-th row being
\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{i}
. Then,

\big\|\bm{W}-\widetilde{\bm{W}}\big\|_{\infty}=\max_{1\leq i\leq m}\big\|\bm{w}_{i}-\widetilde{\bm{w}}_{i}\big\|_{1}\leq u.

Therefore, the
u
-covering number associated with the infinity matrix norm for the class
\mathcal{M}(m,n,r)
satisfies

\mathcal{N}\left(u,\mathcal{M}(m,n,r),\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)\leq(2r/u+1)^{mn},

which completes the proof.
∎

We first define the scaling factors for each layer
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
as
r_{\ell}=\max\{\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{q},1\}
. We then define the new parameters for the rescaled FNN as
\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}=\bm{A}_{\ell}/r_{\ell}
,
\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell}=\bm{b}_{\ell}/(\prod_{s=0}^{\ell}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s}})
,
\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}=\bm{A}_{L}(\prod_{s=0}^{L-1}r_{s}^{m^{L-s}})
and set the new parameterization of
g
as follows:

\displaystyle g=\tilde{g}_{L}\circ\tilde{g}_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ\tilde{g}_{0},\quad\text{ where }

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{\ell}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\bm{x}+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\text{ for }0\leq\ell\leq L-1,\text{ and }

\displaystyle\tilde{g}_{L}(\bm{x})=\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\bm{x}.

For any
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
, due to
r_{s}\geq 1
for
s=0,\dots,L-1
, we have

\|(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell},\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell})\|_{q}=\frac{1}{r_{\ell}}\left\|\left(\bm{A}_{\ell},\frac{\bm{b}_{\ell}}{\prod_{s=0}^{\ell-1}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s}}}\right)\right\|_{q}\leq\frac{1}{r_{\ell}}\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{q}\leq 1.

Next, define
G_{\ell}=g_{\ell}\circ\dots\circ g_{0}
and
\tilde{G}_{\ell}=\tilde{g}_{\ell}\circ\dots\circ\tilde{g}_{0}
for
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
. We show by induction that
G_{\ell}(\bm{x})=(\prod_{s=0}^{\ell}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s+1}})\tilde{G}_{\ell}(\bm{x})
. First, for
\ell=0
, since
\sigma_{m}
is
m
-homogeneous, i.e.
\sigma_{m}(c\bm{x})=c^{m}\sigma_{m}(\bm{x})
, then

G_{0}(\bm{x})=g_{0}(\bm{x})=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{0}\bm{x}+\bm{b}_{0})=r_{0}^{m}\sigma_{m}(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{0}\bm{x}+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{0})=r_{0}^{m}\tilde{g}_{0}(\bm{x})=r_{0}^{m}\tilde{G}_{0}(\bm{x}).

Suppose the claim holds for
\ell-1
, i.e.
G_{\ell-1}(\bm{x})=\left(\prod_{s=0}^{\ell-1}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s}}\right)\widetilde{G}_{\ell-1}(\bm{x})
, then

\displaystyle G_{\ell}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\sigma_{m}(\bm{A}_{\ell}G_{\ell-1}(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell})=\left(r_{\ell}\prod_{s=0}^{\ell-1}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s}}\right)^{m}\sigma_{m}\left(\frac{\bm{A}_{\ell}}{r_{\ell}}\frac{G_{\ell-1}(\bm{x})}{\prod_{s=0}^{\ell-1}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s}}}+\frac{\bm{b}_{\ell}}{r_{\ell}\prod_{s=0}^{\ell-1}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{s=0}^{\ell}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s+1}}\right)\sigma_{m}\left(\tilde{\bm{A}}_{\ell}\tilde{G}_{\ell-1}(\bm{x})+\tilde{\bm{b}}_{\ell}\right)=\left(\prod_{s=0}^{\ell}r_{s}^{m^{\ell-s+1}}\right)\tilde{G}_{\ell}(\bm{x}).

Thus, the claim holds for all
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
. Then, it follows

g(\bm{x})=\bm{A}_{L}G_{L-1}(\bm{x})=\bm{A}_{L}\left(\prod_{s=0}^{L-1}r_{s}^{m^{L-s}}\right)\tilde{G}_{L-1}(\bm{x})=\tilde{\bm{A}}_{L}\tilde{G}_{L-1}(\bm{x}).

Therefore,
g
admits the constructed reparameterization, which completes the proof.
∎

We define the pre-activation vectors
\bm{z}_{0}=\bm{A}_{0}\bm{x}+\bm{b}_{0}
and
\bm{z}_{\ell}=\bm{A}_{\ell}(g_{\ell-1}\circ\dots\circ g_{0})(\bm{x})+\bm{b}_{\ell}
for
1\leq\ell\leq L-1
, and let the
i
-th component of
\bm{z}_{\ell}
be denoted by
z_{\ell,i}
.
The gradient of
g
satisfies

\nabla g(\bm{x})=\bm{A}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{0}\bm{A}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{1}\cdots\bm{A}_{L-1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{L-1}\bm{A}_{L}^{\top}.

Here,
\mathbf{D}_{\ell}=\text{diag}(\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(z_{\ell,1}),\dots,\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(z_{\ell,d_{\ell+1}}))
is the diagonal matrix of activation derivatives at layer
\ell
, where
\sigma_{m}^{\prime}(z)=m(\max\{z,0\})^{m-1}
.
By the sub-multiplicative property of the matrix norm, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla g(\bm{x})\|_{1}=\|\nabla g(\bm{x})^{\top}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\|\bm{A}_{L}\mathbf{D}_{L-1}\bm{A}_{L-1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{0}\bm{A}_{0}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{A}_{L}\right\|_{\infty}\prod_{\ell=0}^{L-1}\left(\left\|\mathbf{D}_{\ell}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{A}_{\ell}\right\|_{\infty}\right).

(33)

We now bound the norm of each term in the above product. Under the parameter norm constraint
\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, it follows that

\|\bm{A}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq\|(\bm{A}_{\ell},\bm{b}_{\ell})\|_{\infty}\leq 1,\qquad\text{for }\;0\leq\ell\leq L-1.

Next, we show by induction that
\|\bm{z}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
.
For
\ell=0
, since
\|\bm{x}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, we have
\|\bm{z}_{0}\|_{\infty}=\|\bm{A}_{0}\bm{x}+\bm{b}_{0}\|_{\infty}\leq\|(\bm{A}_{0},\bm{b}_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Suppose
\|\bm{z}_{\ell-1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. This implies
\|\sigma_{m}(\bm{z}_{\ell-1})\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Then,
\|\bm{z}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}=\|\bm{A}_{\ell}\sigma_{m}(\bm{z}_{\ell-1})+\bm{b}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. Thus, the property holds for all
0\leq\ell\leq L-1.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{D}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}=\max_{i}|m(\max\{z_{\ell,i},0\})^{m-1}|\leq m\cdot 1^{m-1}=m.

Substituting these bounds into (
33
) yields

\displaystyle\|\nabla g(\bm{x})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq m^{L}\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}.

Next, we analyze the Hessian of
g(\bm{x})
, denoted
\nabla^{2}g(\bm{x})
.
The
i
-th column of the Hessian corresponds to the gradient of the partial derivative
\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{x})
.
For any
i\in[d]
, applying the product rule yields a sum of
L
terms:

\nabla\{\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{x})\}=\sum_{j=0}^{L-1}\left(\bm{A}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{0}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{j-1}\bm{A}_{j}^{\top}\right)\left(\frac{\partial\mathbf{D}_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}\right)\left(\bm{A}_{j+1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{j+1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{L-1}\bm{A}_{L}^{\top}\right),

where
\frac{\partial\mathbf{D}_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}
is a diagonal matrix whose
k
-th diagonal entry is

\sigma_{m}^{\prime\prime}(z_{j,k})\cdot\frac{\partial z_{j,k}}{\partial x_{i}}=\sigma_{m}^{\prime\prime}(z_{j,k})\cdot\left(\bm{A}_{j}\mathbf{D}_{j-1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{0}\bm{A}_{0}\right)_{k,i}.

Here,
\sigma_{m}^{\prime\prime}(z)=m(m-1)(\max\{z,0\})^{m-2}
and
(\bm{A})_{k,i}
denotes the
(k,i)
-th entry of
\bm{A}
.
By the triangle inequality and the sub-multiplicative property of the operator norm, it follows

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\{\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{x})\}\big\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{L-1}\big\|\bm{A}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{0}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{j-1}\bm{A}_{j}^{\top}\big\|_{1}\Big\|\frac{\partial\mathbf{D}_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}\Big\|_{1}\big\|\bm{A}_{j+1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{j+1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{L-1}\bm{A}_{L}^{\top}\big\|_{1}.

Then, we bound each part in the product for a given term
j
. From the established bounds
\|\bm{A}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and
\|\mathbf{D}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq m
, for
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{A}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{0}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{j-1}\bm{A}_{j}^{\top}\big\|_{1}\cdot\big\|\bm{A}_{j+1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}_{j+1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{L-1}\bm{A}_{L}^{\top}\big\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\big\|\bm{A}_{j}\mathbf{D}_{j-1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{0}\bm{A}_{0}\big\|_{\infty}\cdot\big\|\bm{A}_{L}\mathbf{D}_{L-1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{j+1}\bm{A}_{j+1}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\prod_{s=0}^{j-1}\|\bm{A}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\mathbf{D}_{s}\|_{\infty}\Big)\|\bm{A}_{j}\|_{\infty}\Big(\prod_{s=j+1}^{L-1}\|\bm{A}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\mathbf{D}_{s}\|_{\infty}\Big)\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m^{L-1}\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}.

For the diagonal matrix, since
\|\bm{z}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
for
0\leq\ell\leq L-1
, then

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{\partial\mathbf{D}_{j}}{\partial x_{i}}\Big\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\max_{k}\big|\sigma_{m}^{\prime\prime}(z_{j,k})\cdot\left(\bm{A}_{j}\mathbf{D}_{j-1}\cdots\mathbf{D}_{0}\bm{A}_{0}\right)_{k,i}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{k}\big|m(m-1)(\max\{z_{j,k},0\})^{m-2}\big|\Big(\prod_{s=0}^{j-1}\|\bm{A}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\mathbf{D}_{s}\|_{\infty}\Big)\|\bm{A}_{j}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq(m-1)m^{j+1}.

Combining these bounds leads that

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\{\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{x})\}\big\|_{1}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{L-1}m^{L-1}\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}(m-1)m^{j+1}\leq Lm^{2L}\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}.

Therefore, we show

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla^{2}g(\bm{x})\big\|_{\infty}=\max_{i}\big\|\nabla\{\partial_{x_{i}}g(\bm{x})\}\big\|_{1}\leq Lm^{2L}\|\bm{A}_{L}\|_{\infty}.

This completes the proof.
∎

By the defintion,

\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}|S_{m}(h)(\bm{x})|\leq 2^{m/2}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}}|h\big(\bm{u}(\bm{x})\big)|\leq 2^{m/2}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}.

Set
r=\|\bm{x}\|_{2}
. A direct chain-rule calculation gives

\nabla_{\bm{x}}S_{m}(h)(\bm{x})=m(1+r^{2})^{m/2-1}\bm{x}h(\bm{u}(\bm{x}))+(1+r^{2})^{m/2}J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u}(\bm{x})),

(34)

where
J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+1)\times d}
is the Jacobian matrix of
\bm{u}(\bm{x})
, which maps vectors from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
. Let
u_{i}(\bm{x})
denote the
i
-th component of
\bm{u}(\bm{x})
. For any
\ell\in[d]
, we have

\frac{\partial u_{i}}{\partial x_{\ell}}=\frac{\delta_{i\ell}(1+r^{2})-x_{i}x_{\ell}}{(1+r^{2})^{3/2}}\quad\text{ for }i\in[d],\qquad\frac{\partial u_{d+1}}{\partial x_{\ell}}=-\frac{x_{\ell}}{(1+r^{2})^{3/2}}.

This implies that

J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{(1+r^{2})^{3/2}}\begin{pmatrix}(1+r^{2})\bm{I}_{d}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{\top}\\[4.0pt]
-\bm{x}^{\top}\end{pmatrix}.

Let
\|J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})\|_{1}
denote the
1
-norm of
J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})
.
Then
\|J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})\|_{1}\leq 2+\sqrt{d}
for any
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{B}^{d}
.

Next, we analyze
\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u})
on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d}
. We will show the following identity holds.

\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u})=-\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\big(u_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}-u_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\big)\,D_{i,j}h(\bm{u})\quad\text{ for }\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}.

(35)

Since
\|u_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}-u_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq|u_{j}|+|u_{i}|\leq\sqrt{2}
, it follows that

\|\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u})\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\|u_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}-u_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\|_{\infty}|D_{i,j}h(\bm{u})|\leq\sqrt{2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\|D_{i,j}h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}.

Combining this with (
34
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{x}}S_{m}(h)(\bm{x})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m(1+r^{2})^{m/2-1}|h(\bm{u}(\bm{x}))|\|\bm{x}\|_{\infty}+(1+r^{2})^{m/2}\,\|J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u}(\bm{x}))\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m2^{m/2-1}|h(\bm{u}(\bm{x}))|+2^{m/2}\|J_{\bm{u}}(\bm{x})\|_{1}\|\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u}(\bm{x}))\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq m2^{m/2-1}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}+2^{(m+1)/2}(2+\sqrt{d})\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\|D_{i,j}h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,d}\Big\{\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\|D_{i,j}h\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{d})}\Big\},

where
C_{m,d}=\max\{m2^{m/2-1},2^{(m+1)/2}(2+\sqrt{d})\}
is a constant depending only on
m
and
d
.

It remains to prove the identity (
35
). To this end, we define
H(\bm{u})=h\big(\tfrac{\bm{u}}{\|\bm{u}\|_{2}}\big)
for any non-zero
\bm{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}
. We then study
\nabla_{\bm{u}}H(\bm{u})
and restrict the gradient to
\mathbb{S}^{d}
. Fix
k\in[d+1]
. We first show that

\partial_{u_{k}}H(\bm{u})=-\sum_{j=1}^{d+1}u_{j}D_{k,j}H(\bm{u})\quad\text{ for }\bm{u}\in\mathbb{S}^{d}.

(36)

By the definition of
D_{k,j}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d+1}u_{j}\,D_{k,j}H=\sum_{j=1}^{d+1}u_{j}\big(u_{k}\partial_{u_{j}}H-u_{j}\partial_{u_{k}}H\big)=u_{k}\sum_{j=1}^{d+1}u_{j}\partial_{u_{j}}H-\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{d+1}u_{j}^{2}\Big)\partial_{u_{k}}H.

(37)

For any
j\in[d+1]
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{u_{j}}H=\sum_{s=1}^{d+1}\partial_{s}h\big(\tfrac{\bm{u}}{\|\bm{u}\|_{2}}\big)\frac{\delta_{sj}\|\bm{u}\|_{2}^{2}-u_{s}u_{j}}{\|\bm{u}\|_{2}^{3}}.

This implies
\bm{u}^{\top}\nabla_{\bm{u}}H(\bm{u})=0
on
\mathbb{S}^{d}
. Since
\|\bm{u}\|_{2}^{2}=1
, the right-hand side of (
37
) reduces to
-\partial_{u_{k}}H
, which verifies (
36
). Next, define

\bm{V}(\bm{u}):=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\big(u_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}-u_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\big)D_{i,j}H(\bm{u}).

Its
k
-th component
V_{k}(\bm{u})
satisfies

\displaystyle V_{k}(\bm{u})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\big(u_{j}\delta_{ik}-u_{i}\delta_{jk}\big)\,D_{i,j}H(\bm{u})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j>k}u_{j}\,D_{k,j}H(\bm{u})-\sum_{i<k}u_{i}\,D_{i,k}H(\bm{u}).

Since
D_{k,i}=-D_{i,k}
for
i<k
and
D_{k,k}=0
, the right-hand side simplifies to

\sum_{j=1}^{d+1}u_{j}\,D_{k,j}H
. By (
36
), it follows
V_{k}(\bm{u})=-\partial_{u_{k}}H(\bm{u})
. Since this holds for every
k\in[d+1]
, we obtain
\bm{V}(\bm{u})=-\nabla_{\bm{u}}H(\bm{u})
. Rearranging the sign and noting that
\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u})=\nabla_{\bm{u}}H(\bm{u})|_{\mathbb{S}^{d}}
yield

\nabla_{\bm{u}}h(\bm{u})=-\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq d+1}\big(u_{j}\mathbf{e}_{i}-u_{i}\mathbf{e}_{j}\big)\,D_{i,j}h(\bm{u}).

This completes the proof.
∎

The star-shaped property of the class implies that for any
r_{1}\leq r_{2}
,

\Big\{f:\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}f\in\mathcal{F},\|f\|_{2}\leq r_{1}\Big\}\subseteq\Big\{f:f\in\mathcal{F},\|f\|_{2}\leq r_{1}\Big\}.

From this, it follows that

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r_{2}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(\bm{x}_{i})\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}\\
\frac{r_{1}}{r_{2}}\|g\|_{2}\leq r_{1}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(\bm{x}_{i})\Big|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}f\in\mathcal{F}\\
\|f\|_{2}\leq r_{1}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}f(\bm{x}_{i})\Big|\leq\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}\\
\|f\|_{2}\leq r_{1}\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}f(\bm{x}_{i})\Big|.

Thus, the proof is completed.
∎

We define a series of random variables indexed by
r
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{n}(r):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\\
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\big|H_{n}(f;f_{0})\big|=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\\
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big\{\ell(Z_{i};f)-\ell(Z_{i};f_{0})\big\}-\mathbb{E}\big[\ell(Z;f)-\ell(Z;f_{0})\big]\Big|.

For any
r>0
, define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,n}(r):=\left\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\frac{\big|H_{n}(f;f_{0})\big|}{\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}+r}>2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r\right\}.

To analyze
\mathcal{I}_{1,n}(r)
, we further define two auxiliary events

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1n}(r):=\Big\{\mathbb{H}_{n}(r)\geq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r^{2}\Big\},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r):=\Big\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\text{ such that }\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\geq r\text{ and }|H_{n}(f;f_{0})|\geq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\Big\}.

Clearly,
\mathcal{I}_{1,n}(r)\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{1n}(r)\cup\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r)
. To see this, we consider two cases.
If there exists
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
such that
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq r
and
\frac{|H_{n}(f;f_{0})|}{\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}+r}>2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r
, then
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n},\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq r}|H_{n}(f;f_{0})|\geq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r^{2}
, thus
\mathcal{A}_{1n}(r)
holds. If there exists
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
such that
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}>r
and
\frac{|H_{n}(f;f_{0})|}{\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}+r}>2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r
, then
|H_{n}(f;f_{0})|>2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}
, thus
\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r)
holds. This confirms
\mathcal{I}_{1,n}(r)\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{1n}(r)\cup\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r)
.

We first bound the probability of
\mathcal{A}_{1n}(r)
. Define
h(\bm{z};f):=\ell(\bm{z};f)-\ell(\bm{z};f_{0}).
Then,
H_{n}(f;f_{0})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\{\mathbb{E}[h(Z;f)]-h(Z_{i};f)\}
. From the Lipschitz property of
\ell
and the uniform boundedness
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|f\|_{\infty}\leq M_{n}
, we have

\sup_{\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}}|h(\bm{z};f)|=\sup_{\bm{z}\in\mathcal{Z}}|\ell(\bm{z};f)-\ell(\bm{z};f_{0})|\leq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}}|f(\bm{x})-f_{0}(\bm{x})|\leq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n},

which implies
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}}\|h(\cdot;f)-\mathbb{E}[h(Z;f)]\|_{\infty}\leq 4\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}.

Furthermore, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
satisfying
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq r
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}\big[\big\{h(Z;f)-\mathbb{E}[h(Z;f)]\big\}^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\{h(Z;f)\}^{2}\big]\leq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}r^{2}.

Additionally, we derive an upper bound for
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{H}_{n}(r)]
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{H}_{n}(r)]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\\
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}h(Z_{i};f)\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 4\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\\
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}(f-f_{0})(X_{i})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 4\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{R}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\Big],

where the first inequality follows from the symmetrization argument, and the second inequality follows from the Ledoux–Talagrand contraction
inequality in Lemma
A.9
.
Since
r_{n}
is selected such that

\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]=r_{n}^{2}/(64M_{n})
, then
for any
s,r\geq r_{n},
it follows from Lemmas
A.7
and
A.10
that

\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{H}_{n}(r)]\leq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}rr_{n}/(16M_{n})
and

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathbb{H}_{n}(r)\geq\frac{\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}}{16M_{n}}rr_{n}+s^{2}\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{ns^{4}}{8e(\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}r^{2}+\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}^{2}rr_{n})+16\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}s^{2}}\Big\}.

By selecting
s^{2}=15\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}rr_{n}/(16M_{n})
, there exists a constant
b_{1}
such that for any
r\geq r_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathbb{H}_{n}(r)\geq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}rr_{n}\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{nb_{1}r^{2}r_{n}^{2}}{M_{n}^{2}(r^{2}+rr_{n})}\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}\Big\}.

(38)

Furthermore, by setting
r=2r_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{1n}(r_{n})\Big\}=P\Big\{\mathbb{H}_{n}(r_{n})\geq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}^{2}\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{2nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{3M_{n}^{2}}\Big\}.

Next, we derive an upper bound for the probability of
\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r),
which is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r):=\Big\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\text{ such that }\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\geq r\text{ and }|H_{n}(f;f_{0})|\geq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\Big\}.

For integers
m\geq 1
, we define a series of events

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{m}(r):=\big\{f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:2^{m-1}r\leq\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}<2^{m}r\big\}.

Setting
r=r_{n}
, we have

\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\leq 2M_{n}<2^{\bar{m}_{n}}r_{n},
where
\bar{m}_{n}=\lceil\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1})/\log 2+1\rceil
. Then,
P\{\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r_{n})\}=\sum_{m=1}^{\bar{m}_{n}}P\{\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r_{n})\cap\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{n})\}
. Now, if the event
\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r_{n})\cap\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{n})
occurs, there exists
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
with
\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}<2^{m}r_{n}:=\tilde{r}_{m}

such that

|H_{n}(f;f_{0})|\geq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}\geq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}\tilde{r}_{m}r_{n}.

From (
38
), we obtain

P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r_{n})\cap\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{n})\Big\}\leq P\Big\{\mathbb{H}_{n}(\tilde{r}_{m})\geq\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}\tilde{r}_{m}r_{n}\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}\Big\}.

Hence, for some constant
b_{2}>0
, it follows

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r_{n})\Big\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{\bar{m}_{n}}P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r_{n})\cap\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{n})\Big\}\leq 2\bar{m}_{n}\exp\Big\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}+b_{2}\log(\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1}))\Big\}.

Combining these results, we show that the probability of
\mathcal{I}_{1,n}(r_{n})
can be bounded as

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathcal{I}_{1,n}(r_{n})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{1n}(r_{n})\Big\}+P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{2n}(r_{n})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{2nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{3M_{n}^{2}}\Big\}+\exp\Big\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}+b_{2}\log(\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1}))\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}+b_{2}\log(\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1}))\Big\}.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-2\exp\{-\frac{nb_{1}r_{n}^{2}}{2M_{n}^{2}}+b_{2}\log(\log(M_{n}r_{n}^{-1}))\}
, we derive

\big|H_{n}(f;f_{0})\big|\leq 2\|\ell\|_{\operatorname{Lip}}M_{n}^{-1}r_{n}(\|f-f_{0}\|_{2}+r_{n})~~~~\text{ for any $f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}$},

which completes the proof.
∎

We first show the relationship between
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]
and
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})
. For any
r>0
, define

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r):=\left\{\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}}\frac{\big|\|g\|_{2}^{2}-\|g\|_{2,n}^{2}\big|}{\|g\|_{2}^{2}+r^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\right\}.

On the complement of
\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r)
, for every
g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
, it holds that

\|g\|_{2,n}\leq 2\|g\|_{2}+r,\quad\text{ and }\quad\|g\|_{2}\leq 2\|g\|_{2,n}+r.

In this scenario, the following bounds for
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})
and
\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})
can be derived.

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(X_{i})\Big|\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2,n}\leq 3r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(X_{i})\Big|\Big]=\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(3r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}).

For any
r,s>0
, we define the event

\mathcal{A}_{2,n}(r,s):=\Big\{\big|\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})-\mathbb{E}\big[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\big]\big|\geq s\Big\}.

On the complement of
\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r)\cup\mathcal{A}_{2,n}(r,s)
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\big[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]\leq\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(3r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})+s.\end{split}

(39)

Subsequent analysis focuses on bounding the probabilities of
\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r)
and
\mathcal{A}_{2,n}(r,s)
.

First, we consider the event
\mathcal{A}_{2,n}(r,s)
. Let

\displaystyle m(X_{1},\dots,X_{n}):=\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})=\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(X_{i})\Big|\Big].

Since
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
is uniformly bounded by
2M_{n}
,
m(\cdot)
satisfies the bounded differences property. Specifically, if two samples
\{X_{1},\dots,X_{n}\}
and
\{\tilde{X}_{1},\dots,\tilde{X}_{n}\}
differ in at most one element,

\displaystyle\Big|m(X_{1},\dots,X_{n})-m(\tilde{X}_{1},\dots,\tilde{X}_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(X_{i})\Big|\Big]-\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(\tilde{X}_{i})\Big|\Big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\sigma}}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}\big\{g(X_{i})-g(\tilde{X}_{i})\big\}\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big|g(X_{i})-g(\tilde{X}_{i})\big|\leq\frac{4M_{n}}{n}.

Applying McDiarmid’s inequality from Lemma
A.11
, we obtain

\displaystyle P\Big\{\Big|\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})-\mathbb{E}[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]\Big|\geq s\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{ns^{2}}{8M_{n}^{2}}\Big\}.

Therefore, for any
r,s>0
, there holds

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{2,n}(r,s)\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{-\frac{ns^{2}}{8M_{n}^{2}}\Big\}.

(40)

Next, to analyze the event
\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r)=\big\{\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}}|\|g\|_{2}^{2}-\|g\|_{2,n}^{2}|(\|g\|_{2}^{2}+r^{2})^{-1}\geq\frac{1}{2}\big\},

we define a series of random variables
\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)
and events
\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)
, given as

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\|g\|_{2}^{2}-\|g\|_{2,n}^{2}\Big|\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)=\Big\{\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)\geq r^{2}/2\Big\}.

We first establish that
\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r)\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)
. Specifically, consider any
g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
satisfying
\|g\|_{2}\leq r
and
|\|g\|_{2}^{2}-\|g\|_{2,n}^{2}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|g\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}r^{2}
. In this case,
\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)
clearly holds. Now, consider
g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
such that
\|g\|_{2}>r
and
|\|g\|_{2}^{2}-\|g\|_{2,n}^{2}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\|g\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}r^{2}
. Let
\tilde{g}=\frac{r}{\|g\|_{2}}g
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
is star-shaped,
\tilde{g}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
. Furthermore, we have
\|\tilde{g}\|_{2}=r
and
|\|\tilde{g}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\tilde{g}\|_{2,n}^{2}|\geq\frac{1}{2}r^{2}
, which demonstrates that
\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)
also holds in this case. Thus,
\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r)\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)
is verified. Consequently, it suffices to analyze the event
\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)
.

To bound the probability of
\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)
, note that
\sup_{g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}}\|g^{2}-\mathbb{E}[g^{2}(X)]\|_{\infty}\leq 8M_{n}^{2}
and

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|g^{2}(X)-\mathbb{E}[g^{2}(X)]\big|^{2}\big]\leq\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\big[|g(X)|^{4}\big]\leq 4M_{n}^{2}r^{2}.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)]\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g^{2}(X_{i})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 16M_{n}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}\\
\|g\|_{2}\leq r\end{subarray}}\Big|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sigma_{i}g(X_{i})\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle=16M_{n}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{R}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\Big],

where the first inequality follows from the symmetrization argument, and the second inequality follows from the Ledoux–Talagrand contraction
inequality in Lemma
A.9
.
Since
\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]/r
is a non-increasing function for
r\in(0,\infty)
, as established in Lemma
A.7
, then for any
r\geq r_{n}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{R}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\Big]\leq\frac{r}{r_{n}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\Big].

From Talagrand concentration in Lemma
A.10
, for any
r\geq r_{n}
and any
x>0
, we have

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)\geq\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)\big]+x\Big\}\leq 2\exp\bigg\{\frac{-nx^{2}}{8e(4M_{n}^{2}r^{2}+16^{2}M_{n}^{3}rr_{n}^{-1}\mathbb{E}[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})])+32M_{n}^{2}x}\bigg\}.

(41)

As
r_{n}
is chosen to satisfy

\mathbb{E}\Big[\overline{R}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\Big]=\frac{r_{n}^{2}}{64M_{n}},

(42)

it follows that
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)]\leq rr_{n}/4
, and the selection
x=r^{2}/4
further leads (
41
) to

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathbb{G}_{n}(r)\geq\frac{rr_{n}}{4}+\frac{r^{2}}{4}\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{\frac{-nr^{4}}{a_{2}M_{n}^{2}(r^{2}+rr_{n})}\Big\},

where
a_{2}
is a positive constant. Therefore, for any
r\geq r_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle P\Big\{\mathcal{A}_{1,n}(r)\Big\}\leq P\Big\{\mathcal{B}_{n}(r)\Big\}\leq 2\exp\Big\{\frac{-nr^{4}}{a_{2}M_{n}^{2}(r^{2}+rr_{n})}\Big\}.

(43)

Combining (
39
), (
40
), and (
43
), for any
r\geq r_{n}
and any
s\geq 0
, with
r_{n}
satisfying (
42
), then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]\leq\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(3r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})+s,

with probability at least
1-2\exp\{-\frac{ns^{2}}{8M_{n}^{2}}\}-2\exp\{-\frac{nr^{4}}{a_{2}M_{n}^{2}(r^{2}+rr_{n})}\}
. Set
r=r_{n}
and
s=r_{n}^{2}/(128M_{n})
. If
r_{n}\geq\hat{r}_{n}
, then by a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
A.7
,

\displaystyle\frac{r_{n}^{2}}{64M_{n}}=\mathbb{E}\big[\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})]\leq\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(3r_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})+\frac{r_{n}^{2}}{128M_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq 3r_{n}\hat{r}_{n}^{-1}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(\hat{r}_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})+\frac{r_{n}^{2}}{128M_{n}}

\displaystyle\leq 3cr_{n}\hat{r}_{n}+\frac{r_{n}^{2}}{128M_{n}}.

This implies that
\hat{r}_{n}\geq a_{3}r_{n}/(cM_{n})
for some constant
a_{3}>0
, with probability at least
1-2\exp\{-\frac{nr_{n}^{4}}{a_{4}M_{n}^{4}}\}-2\exp\{-\frac{nr_{n}^{2}}{2a_{2}M_{n}^{2}}\}
for some constant
a_{4}>0
. If additionally
r_{n}/M_{n}=o(1)
, then for large
n
, the bound holds with probability at least
1-\exp\{-\frac{nr_{n}^{4}}{a_{5}M_{n}^{4}}\}
for some constant
a_{5}>0.
By appropriately adjusting constants, the proof is completed.
∎

We first derive a bound on the covering number of
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
.
For any
u>0
, let
\{f_{1},\dots,f_{q}\}
be an
(u/2)
-cover of
\mathcal{F}_{n}
, and let
\{a_{1},\dots,a_{s}\}
be an
(u/4M_{n})
-cover of
[0,1]
. Then, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
and
a\in[0,1]
,

\|a(f-f_{0})-a_{j}(f_{i}-f_{0})\|_{\infty}\leq|a-a_{j}|\|f-f_{0}\|_{\infty}+a_{j}\|f-f_{i}\|_{\infty}.

Thus
\{a_{j}(f_{i}-f_{0}):j=1,\dots,s,i=1,\dots,q\}
forms a
u
-cover of
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*}
. This implies

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*},L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}))

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq\log\mathcal{N}(u/2,\mathcal{F}_{n},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})+\log\mathcal{N}(u/(4M_{n}),[0,1],|\cdot|\;)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{S}\log(1+Ku^{-1})+\log(1+M_{n}u^{-1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{S}\log(1+Ku^{-1}),

where the third inequality follows from Theorem 3.3 in the main text and Lemma
A.1
, and the last inequality follows from the condition
M_{n}\lesssim K
. By Dudley’s integral covering number bound from Lemma
A.8
, we derive

\begin{split}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})&\leq\inf_{\eta\geq 0}\left\{4\eta+12\int_{\eta}^{r}\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}(u,\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*},L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}))}{n}}du\right\}\\
&\leq\inf_{\eta\geq 0}\left\{4\eta+12r\sqrt{\frac{\log\mathcal{N}(\eta,\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*},L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}))}{n}}\right\}\\
&\lesssim\inf_{\eta\geq 0}\left\{4\eta+12r\sqrt{\frac{\mathcal{S}\log(1+K\eta^{-1})}{n}}\right\}.\end{split}

Choosing
\eta=rn^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}\log(K)}
yields

\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(r;\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\;\lesssim\;rn^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}\big(\log(Kn)+\log(r^{-1})\big)}.

Define
\hat{r}_{n}=n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)}.
Then there exists a constant
b_{1}>0
such that

\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n}(\hat{r}_{n};\mathcal{F}_{n}^{*})\;\leq\;b_{1}\hat{r}_{n}^{2}.

Further, Lemma
A.13
implies that

r_{n}\leq b_{1}s_{1}^{-1}M_{n}n^{-1/2}\sqrt{\mathcal{S}\log(Kn)}.

Setting
c=b_{1}s_{1}^{-1}
completes the proof.
∎

It suffices to show the subadditivity property

(x+y)^{r}\leq x^{r}+y^{r}\quad\text{for any }x,y>0,\ r\in(0,1].

Without loss of generality, assume
a\geq b
. Then

a^{\,r}-b^{\,r}=(b+(a-b))^{\,r}-b^{\,r}\leq(a-b)^{\,r}.

To verify the subadditivity, define
g(t)=(1+t)^{r}-1-t^{r}
for
t\geq 0.

We have
g(0)=0
, and
g^{\prime}(t)=r((1+t)^{\,r-1}-t^{\,r-1}).

Since
r-1<0
, the function
s\mapsto s^{\,r-1}
is strictly decreasing, and
1+t>t
for
t>0
, we have
g^{\prime}(t)<0
.
Thus
g(t)\leq 0
for all
t\geq 0
, which implies

(1+t)^{r}\leq 1+t^{r}.

Let
t=y/x
and multiply both sides by
x^{r}
to obtain

(x+y)^{r}\leq x^{r}+y^{r}.

This completes the proof.

∎

E
Algorithm

Algorithm
1
implements WDRO using a dual perturbation strategy that systematically generates adversarial examples by perturbing both covariates and responses. At each iteration, it applies the Frank-Wolfe method
[
18
]
to identify the optimal covariate perturbation that maximizes the loss, followed by a response perturbation based on the gradient information with respect to the updated covariates. This procedure follows the framework proposed by Staib et al.
[
48
]
, where the Frank-Wolfe method is employed to efficiently approximate the worst-case distribution of covariates for solving the distributionally robust optimization problem.

Algorithm 1

WDRO with perturbations on both covariates and responses

0:

Dataset
(X,Y)
, model
f_{\theta}
, uncertainty level
\delta
, loss
L
, step size
\gamma_{t}=\frac{2}{t+2}

1:

for
each iteration
t=0,1,\ldots,T

do

2:

Sample mini-batch
(x,y)\sim\mathbb{P}_{t}

3:

Initialize
x_{\text{adv}}\leftarrow x

4:

for
each inner step
k=0,1,\ldots,K

do

5:

Compute loss
\ell=\mathbb{E}[L(f_{\theta}(x_{\text{adv}}),y)]

6:

Compute gradients
\nabla_{x}\ell

7:

Find
\delta_{x}^{*}=\argmax_{\delta_{x}:\|\delta_{x}\|_{t,p}\leq\delta/2}\delta_{x}^{\top}\nabla_{x}\ell

8:

Covariate perturbation:
x_{\text{adv}}\leftarrow(1-\gamma_{k})x_{\text{adv}}+\gamma_{k}(x_{\text{adv}}+\delta_{x}^{*})

9:

end

for

10:

Compute loss
\ell=\mathbb{E}[L(f_{\theta}(x_{\text{adv}}),y)]

11:

Compute gradients
\nabla_{y}\ell

12:

Find
\delta_{y}^{*}=\argmax_{\delta_{y}:\|\delta_{y}\|_{t,p}\leq\delta/2}\delta_{y}^{\top}\nabla_{y}\ell

13:

Response perturbation:
y_{\text{adv}}\leftarrow y+\delta_{y}^{*}

14:

Update
\theta
using adversarial loss
\mathbb{E}[L(f_{\theta}(x_{\text{adv}}),y_{\text{adv}})]

15:

end

for
